# @graview/primitives

## 0.1.64

### Patch Changes

- 61c632e: An app's own images are drawn under its base, its head offers home-screen icons, and a dark app opens through a dark screen, Roadmap f-brand-marks-under-app-base (Graview Cloud's brief b-cloud-brand-marks-base-2026-10-29), f-app-icons-home-screen and f-splash-follows-declared-scheme, all three from TECH N9NE on Graview Cloud.
  
  A document names its logo, its page icon and its opening screen's image at `/graview/assets/<sha>.<ext>` on the host's root, and every face drew them there, so on a host that serves each app under a base (`https://<workspace>.graview.app/a/<app>/`) a visitor's logo and icon found nothing. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider`, the Shell and the routed face (`PagesApp`'s context) now take `assetsAt?: (path) => string`, as `filesAt` already says where a record's files are served: every path under `/graview/assets/` the page draws is asked of it — the app bar's and the masthead's mark (each scheme's face), the page's icon, the logo a worker view is handed, and an image a worker view draws by its path where the view's definition says no `asset` of its own. Inline SVG, an emoji, a page-relative path and any other address are drawn as given, and unsaid nothing changes. `assetSrc(assetsAt, href)` and `HOST_ASSETS` on `@graview/core` say it once; `useHostAssets` and `HostAssetsContext` on `@graview/react/provider` carry it to a design's own shell. `splashHtml` takes `assetsAt` too, for the screen's image and the plain screen's mark. The static page renderer already drew a host-root mark under its `base`, and still does. Core's `a-mark-under-an-app-base-is-drawn-there.test.ts` and primitives' `a-logo-under-an-app-base-is-drawn-there.test.tsx` hold it, and verify-brand opens the app at `/a/en-dash/` with its own images kept only there, as a fresh visitor, on both faces in three engines (aLogoUnderAnAppBaseLoadsForAVisitor).
  
  The page's head had an icon link and nothing a phone keeps an app by. A new entry, `@graview/core/head` (on no page up front but `themeColorsOf`), says it from the brand and the base: `homeScreenOf(brand, { base, assetsAt?, name?, png? })` answers the head's tags as HTML — the icon typed by what it is (a PNG is `image/png`, never served as an SVG), an `apple-touch-icon` at 180, `<link rel="manifest">`, and a `theme-color` per scheme with its `media`, one with none where the brand pins a scheme — the web manifest (`name`, `short_name`, 192, 512 and maskable 512 icons, `theme_color` and `background_color` from the brand's ground, `display: "standalone"`, `start_url` and `scope` at the base) and the files a host answers under the base, each saying what it is made from. A PNG mark is every size as it is, or resized by the host; an SVG or emoji mark is no PNG without a rasterizer, so unless the host says `png: true` (it makes them) it is offered as SVG alone — `/icon.svg` and the manifest's `sizes: "any"` — and no `apple-touch-icon` is written. `graview serve` and `graview preview` answer the manifest and `/icon.svg` and send a PNG size to the brand's own PNG (`homeScreenAnswer` on `@graview/ship` and `/runtime`), and `graview preview` writes the tags into its page. A face that owns the page — the Shell, the routed face on its own, an embed told `favicon: true` — puts the app's `theme-color` in the head while it is drawn (`useThemeColor`, on `@graview/primitives`), the page's own put back after. Core's `the-head-offers-home-screen-icons.test.ts` and ship's `a-served-app-offers-its-home-screen-icons.test.ts` hold it, and verify-preview opens a preview whose head offers the icon, the manifest and its every icon each answered as what it says, and the app's one color (theHeadOffersHomeScreenIcons).
  
  TECH N9NE's brand says `scheme: "dark"`, and on a light device its opening screen drew a cream veil with dark ink before the app opened dark. `splashHtml` now opens in a scheme the reader chose where the host says `light` or `dark`; else the one the app declares — the brand's `scheme`, else the screen's own — and a host's `scheme: "auto"` no longer overrides it; else the device's (`splashScheme` says which). `brand.splash.scheme` — `"light"`, `"dark"` or `"device"` — is the screen's own where the brand pins none; `set-brand` takes it and says it ("The app opens on a dark editorial screen while it loads."), and `graview check` says one the brand's scheme overrides (`brand-splash-scheme`, a warning). Core's `an-opening-screen-says-its-scheme.test.ts` and pages' `a-dark-app-opens-through-a-dark-screen.test.ts` hold it, and verify-preview opens a dark app on a device that prefers light: the screen's ground is dark and its words light before the app's code ran, and the app opens dark (aDarkAppOpensThroughADarkSplashOnALightDevice). The graview-logo skill says the base and the home screen, graview-brand the screen's scheme and graview-host-page `assetsAt` in a line.
  
  A hosted page carries 1.4 KB more up front (1_398 bytes minified, 477 gzipped) and a page handed a compiled app 1.3 KB more (1_358 / 471): `assetsAt` through the provider and the frame, `assetSrc`, and the theme-color a face puts in its head, under raises naming this changeset (`scripts/budget-raises/brand-marks-under-an-app-base-and-home-screen-icons-*.json`); the pages face alone 1_434 / 528 more, every face 834 gzipped, the embed with the studio handed in 726 gzipped, the guest host registering a worker view 361 / 166, and Cloud's worker 250 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_097_638 B (1072 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged; `graview serve` and `graview preview` answer `/manifest.webmanifest` and `/icon.svg`, and a PNG size with a 307 to the brand's PNG, where they answered nothing. The declaration and check finding codes — `brand.splash` gains the optional key `scheme`, which a build before this one refuses by path, and one new warning, `brand-splash-scheme`; no declaration that passed is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `set-brand`'s `splash` takes `scheme`. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider`, the Shell, the routed face's context and `splashHtml` gain the optional `assetsAt`; `splashHtml`'s `scheme: "auto"` stops overriding a scheme the app declares.
  
  What Graview Cloud does: for the marks, passes `assetsAt` — the path under `/a/<slug>` — to `mount`, so the app bar's logo and the page's icon load under the app's base, and to `splashHtml`, in place of its own `underBase` rewriting of the splash's image and logo. For the home screen, it writes `homeScreenOf(brand, { base: "/a/<slug>", png: true })`'s `html` into the app page's head and answers its `files` from its worker — the manifest's JSON, `/icon.svg`, and each PNG resized from the brand's PNG or rasterized from its SVG or emoji (`from`), in place of `favicon.svg` serving PNG bytes; serving those files is Cloud's half. For the opening screen, `splashHtml` already draws TECH N9NE's editorial screen dark; Cloud's own `.gc-splash` screen (its owner's shader and its frame) follows `prefers-color-scheme` alone, and should open in `splashScheme(brand)` instead — a `data-scheme` and `u_light` from it — and its opening-screen form may offer `scheme` where the brand pins none.

## 0.1.63

### Patch Changes

- c91af18: A record's page draws a blank fact an act the seat may run writes as an empty slot, Roadmap request:record-pages-draw-writable-but-blank-facts-as-empty-editable-slots. A placement made without its allocation or its role had nowhere on its own page to be given them: a blank fact was not drawn, and the act that writes it (`edit-placement`) surfaces only as the dashed editor on a fact, never as a button — so a new record's empty field could be set only from a form somewhere else. Now each field with no value that an act the seat may run writes — taking the value and asking nothing else — stands where the kind's page orders it (`display.page`, `set-page-fields`) as its label and a dim "add", a button named "Add Allocation" that the keyboard reaches; pressing it opens the same in-place editor a value opens, a choice opening on no choice, and saving fills the fact. A blank no act writes (a `fixed` field) stays unsaid, and a seat the policy keeps from the act — a reader — is offered no slot; a file field is still filled where its files are put (FR-163), and another app's address or a record in one is still picked where it is drawn. Underneath, `readableFields` takes `blanks` — the fields to say as empty slots, never on a glance — and a `ReadableField` read that way carries `blank: true`; `recordFacts` passes `blanks` through, the derived record page asks `blankWritable(store, id, principal)` for them where values are changed in place, `RecordField` draws the slot, and `EditableValue` takes `blank`. Pages' `a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot.test.tsx` and core's `a-blank-a-page-may-fill-is-said-where-it-stands.test.ts` hold it, and a new harness, `verify-blank-facts`, opens a staffing firm's placement in three engines and finds the slot by Tab, fills it by Enter, and finds no slot for the fixed field or for a reader (aBlankFieldAnActWritesIsAnEmptySlot, aBlankFieldNoActWritesStaysHidden, aReaderSeesNoEmptySlot). And a filled value is drawn as a control only where an act the seat may run writes it, Roadmap request:in-place-editors-ignore-grants: the in-place editors offered every act that wrote a field whatever the seat's grants, so a reader saw dashed values the store would refuse; `editableFields` now takes `{ principal }` and offers only the acts `store.permittedMutations` gives it, `useEditableFields` passes the seat's, and a reader's values are read-only on every face — as are everyone's under a provider handed no seat over a policy, which the store would refuse anyway (the same check the blank slots make; aReaderSeesNoEditableValue in `verify-blank-facts`). The graview-pages skill says it in one sentence.
  
  A hosted page's first load stays within its budget. The pages face alone carries 1_329 / 463 more, a visitor's form alone 225 / 83, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 225 / 88, every face 529 gzipped and the embed with the studio handed in 520 gzipped, each under a raise of its own (`scripts/budget-raises/a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot-*.json`); Cloud's worker, which had no room, 600 bytes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The look: a record's page draws a blank writable fact as `[data-graview-blank-fact="<field>"]` with a `button[data-graview-blank]` named "Add <label>". What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a reader's values stop looking editable, a placement's allocation is set from its own page, and a host that drew its own "add" for a blank field can take it away.
- 511184d: A person proposes changes and a reviewer accepts them, Roadmap f-proposals-for-review. Only the app's own actor could leave a change waiting for somebody: a helper who could see a task and was not trusted to close it met a struck-through act and a sentence, and an agent acting for them could do nothing at all.
  
  A grant may now say what its roles may propose and not run: `{ roles: ["helper"], mutations: [], propose: ["close-task"] }` (acts by name or `"*"`, on the grant's `kinds`; a grant that only proposes says `mutations: []`). Reviewing takes no grant of its own: whoever may run the calls decides. `proposes(policy, principal, act, kind)` and `store.mayPropose(call, principal)` say whether a seat may propose a call it may not run; `proposedMutations` is on `@graview/core/tidying`.
  
  `propose(store, principal, calls, intent)` on `@graview/core/tidying` keeps the calls as the same suggestion op the app's own actor makes, with the person as its author and a lineage naming them (`proposedBy`), its basis their words, else "Proposed by Ada". It travels as a call of the store's own `propose-changes` act (`PROPOSE_CHANGES`, `{ calls, intent? }`), which every store answers to and no list offers, and the store makes its op itself: refused, in a sentence, where the seat may neither run nor propose one of the acts, and each call rehearsed as the store would judge it for the reviewer — the act's own guard, the rules, what the seat may see. The same change already waiting is the one that waits. Whoever may run the calls accepts it (`acceptSuggestion`), made as themselves, the lineage naming the proposal and its proposer (`suggestion`, `proposedBy`); a decline answers it in words ("Declined Ada's proposal: …"); its proposer withdraws it (`withdrawSuggestion`). Accepted, it lands in the working state, and reaches readers on the next publish where the app publishes. `isProposal(suggestion)` tells a person's proposal from the app's own suggestion.
  
  An act's form whose seat may propose it and not run it reads "Propose" and keeps the change as the seat's proposal ("Proposed: it waits in Suggested for somebody who may make it."); a record's page offers such an act beside the rest rather than striking it through. The Suggested tray lists a person's proposals apart and first, under "Proposed", each "Proposed by Ada:", with Withdraw in place of a vote for its proposer. An agent acting for somebody who may propose is offered `propose_changes` (`{ calls: { act, args }[], intent? }`), and `list_suggestions` says `proposedBy`. The studio keeps a grant's `propose`. The permissions and tidying skills say how.
  
  The store's own "Propose changes", the proposal it makes, a grant's `propose` and the form's "Propose" cost a hosted page 2.7 KB more up front (2_813 bytes minified, 834 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 2.4 KB (2_492 / 737), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 4_120 / 1_191 more, a visitor's form alone 3_909 / 1_127, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 2_968 / 940, every face 7_378 / 2_234, the embed with the studio handed in 6_430 / 1_993 and Cloud's worker 10_754 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_096_224 B (1071 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — a suggestion's op may now have a person as its author, made by the built-in `propose-changes` act, its lineage naming them as `proposedBy` (1–120 characters, which `lineageOf` holds it to); no primitive is new, and accepting, declining and withdrawing are the ops they were. Stored formats — the one lineage key, and nothing moves: a build before this one reads a proposal as any suggestion and keeps `proposedBy` as a key it does not know. The wire — unchanged in shape: a proposal travels as a `call` of `propose-changes`, which a server that predates it refuses as an act it does not have. The declaration and check finding codes — a grant may say `propose`, `grant-act` reads it too (at `policy.grants.<n>.propose`), `add-grant` and `remove-grant` take it, and a grant may name no act to run where it names acts to propose; nothing that compiled before is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — `propose_changes` is new, listed only for a seat whose policy lets it propose an act it may not run, so a surface whose policy names no `propose` keeps its tools and its hash; `list_suggestions` carries `proposedBy` on a proposal. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build, lets an owner say who may propose in its permissions editor, and draws its review queue from `suggestions()` with `isProposal`, accepting as the reviewer and declining or withdrawing with the functions above.
- 52aed08: A shared or signed-out seat cannot vote on a suggestion, Roadmap f-votes-shared-seat (from Cloud's asks after 0.1.62: the shared anonymous seat's votes, advisory votes, the tray's "Sign in to vote" and the away line's tally). A host that serves every signed-out visitor as one principal — Cloud's `anonymous` — got one shared vote on 0.1.62: a stranger's `POST /graview/ops` was kept as that seat's vote, the next stranger flipped it, and the tray showed one stranger another's. Now a principal says it is nobody in particular with `shared: true` (`Principal.shared`), and the store refuses a vote on a suggestion from such a seat, from a `visitor`, or from an agent acting for either, in the words "Sign in to vote on this suggestion." — through `permits`, so Accept and Decline, `voteSuggestion`, a raw call, the wire, a socket and an RPC all meet it, in an app with a policy or without one. A host that wants strangers' sentiment says `votes: { advisory: "visitors" }` on the store (`StoreOptions.votes`, the handler's and `serve`'s `votes`, `openRemote`'s `storeOptions`) and names each visitor behind the shared seat with `visitorKey`: such a vote is kept with `advisory: true` and `voter` (that key) on its op, one standing vote per visitor, counted apart in `tally.advisory: { for, against }`, never toward `autoAcceptAt`, a visitor's own in `tally.mine`, and only that visitor's to undo — a shared seat naming no visitor is still refused. `signedOutSeat`, `advisoryVoterOf` and `SIGN_IN_TO_VOTE` are on `@graview/core/tidying`, `VotesOptions` on the main entry; `votesIn(ops, { advisory: true })` reads the advisory votes alone. The Suggested tray asks the store before a press: a seat whose vote would be refused sees the host's `signIn` as one native button, "Sign in to vote", where Accept and Decline were, and no vote at all where the host gave no way in; whoever may decide reads visitors apart, "1 for, 0 against · 2 visitors agree". `AwaySummary` takes `agreed`, and `whileAway` counts it — the most people whose vote stands for one waiting suggestion — said "3 readers agree with one that waits". `a-shared-or-signed-out-seat-votes-only-where-the-host-keeps-its-vote-advisory.test.ts` (core), `a-strangers-vote-over-the-wire-is-refused-unless-the-host-keeps-it-advisory.test.ts` (ship), `a-visitor-is-offered-sign-in-rather-than-a-vote.test.tsx` (primitives) and `while-away-says-how-many-agree-with-one-that-waits.test.ts` (tools) hold it, and the chrome harness opens the tray on both faces as the host's shared signed-out seat and finds Sign in to vote in place of a vote, pressing it asking the host and keeping nothing (aVisitorIsOfferedSignInRatherThanAVote). The graview-tidying skill says it.
  
  A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front (717 bytes minified, 220 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 0.7 KB: 711 and 236): who is anybody in particular and an advisory vote's place in the tally — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-shared-seat-cannot-vote-*.json` say each entry's bytes; the pages face alone 717 / 215 more, a visitor's form alone 717 / 225, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 723 / 240, every face 1_796 / 656, the embed with the studio handed in 1_033 / 356 and Cloud's worker 2_166 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_078_694 B (1054 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — a vote op from a shared or signed-out seat (`shared: true`, a `visitor`, or an agent acting for one) is now refused where it was kept, unless the host keeps it advisory; an advisory vote's op carries two new optional keys on `vote`, `advisory: true` and `voter`, which a build before it keeps and reads as a vote of the shared seat's; no primitive is added. Stored formats — nothing moves; a log of 0.1.62's shared-seat votes folds and counts as before. The wire — unchanged in shape; such a vote is refused as any act is, with its sentence. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged; `votes` is the host's, on the store, not the document's. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `list_suggestions`' `tally` may carry `advisory`. What Graview Cloud does: mark its signed-out principal `shared: true` in `seatOf`, and drop its own refusal or keep it; to gather strangers' sentiment, hand `votes: { advisory: "visitors" }` to the store and the remote's `storeOptions` and a per-visitor `visitorKey` (its visitor cookie) on the seat, and count `tally.advisory` in its tidying notice apart; hand the page `signIn` for a signed-out reader so the tray says Sign in to vote; pass `agreed` from `whileAway` in `AwaySummary`.
- bd2a06c: A workflow sets aside what is done with, asks about a run as one suggestion, and follows rules its people keep as records, Roadmap f-retention-rules, f-job-approvals and f-job-rule-inputs. A done grocery list stayed in every list until somebody archived it by hand, a workflow whose changes were to be approved first left one suggestion per change in the Suggested tray — twenty-eight rows for one Sunday's events — and the rules an owner wanted a workflow to keep lived in its prompt, where only the author could change them.
  
  A workflow's step may now be `{ "archive": "grocery-list", "where": "status == 'done'", "after": "3 days" }` (or `"after": { "field": "doneOn", "days": 7 }`): each record of the kind the rule holds of, once it has stood that long since it last changed or after the date it holds, is archived through its kind's own `archive-<kind>` — never removed — one change per record under the run's lineage, so Activity says the run as one row. It passes by a record a person said to leave alone, every record of a kind the app says is never tidied, a record a person brought back after this workflow archived it (an undo of its archive, or "Bring it back"), and a record a rule it follows keeps, and says each. No model is asked. The checker says `workflow-kind-unknown`, `workflow-where-invalid`, `workflow-archive-never` (a kind that says `archives: false`) and `workflow-archive-after-invalid` (a wait that does not read as hours, days or weeks, or a field the kind does not have). `archiveStep`, `afterMs` and `afterWords` are on `@graview/core/workflows`, `runArchiveStep` on `@graview/tools/workflows`.
  
  A workflow whose `approve` is "ask" now gathers its whole run into ONE suggestion (`askedOf`, `@graview/tools/workflows`): each change rehearsed as it is asked — refused there if the store would refuse it, left out if it changes nothing or a person declined it — and kept as one op whose `batchIntent` says the run as one ("Fill out next week's events: 28 events to add, 3 to change, 1 to archive", `proposalWords` on `@graview/core/tidying`), whose `suggestsSaid` keeps each call's own sentence, and whose lineage names the run. A later run's group takes the place of the one still waiting (withdrawn, which is no decline); a run that asks exactly what still waits leaves it standing. The tray says the run once after who suggests it, opens to "Show the 28 changes", each in its act's sentence with its own Accept (`Accept: <sentence>`); the row's "Accept all" takes what still waits and Decline the rest. `acceptSuggestion(store, id, { items })` takes some of a suggestion's calls by their places, its lineage naming them (`lineage.items`), and the rest wait; `suggestions()` lists what waits as `items: { at, call, intent }[]` with the maker's `headline`; a decline after a partial acceptance declines only what was not taken. A reader votes on the whole, and is offered no change of their own. An edit of a change before accepting it is not in this build.
  
  A workflow may say `"follows": "<kind>"`: the app's own records of that kind are rules it follows, read by one convention — `action` (or `rule`, `instruction`, `text`; the record's label when none) the rule verbatim, `order` (or `rank`, `priority`) lower first, `appliesTo` a kind's name or plural or a rule-language condition read on each record, and `status` retired, off, inactive, paused, draft or archived (or `active: false`) to set one aside. An agent step is handed the rules that stand, numbered, after its instruction; an archive step passes by every record a rule whose action begins "keep" or "never archive" applies to. `policiesOf`, `policyApplies`, `policyInstruction`, `keeps` and `followsFindings` (`workflow-follows-unknown`) are on `@graview/core/workflows`.
  
  A lineage that names which of a suggestion's changes a person took, a decline that leaves those out, and the tray's list of a run's changes cost a hosted page 0.3 KB more up front (338 bytes minified, 92 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.3 KB (338 / 111), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 338 / 95 more, a visitor's form alone 338 / 104, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 338 / 116, every face 5_909 / 1_889 the embed with the studio handed in 4_095 / 1_378 and Cloud's worker 6_694 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_079_378 B (1055 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — two optional additions and no primitive: a suggestion's op may carry `suggestsSaid`, and a lineage may name `items`, which `lineageOf` holds to at most 500 whole numbers; an op without either reads and folds as before, and a build before this one keeps both, lists the suggestion's calls whole and reads a partial acceptance as the whole suggestion taken. Stored formats — the same two fields, and nothing moves. The wire — unchanged in shape: a partial acceptance travels as a `call` with its lineage. The declaration and check finding codes — a workflow step may be `archive`, a workflow may say `follows`, and `workflow-archive-never`, `workflow-archive-after-invalid` and `workflow-follows-unknown` are new; nothing that compiled before is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its tray draws a run's suggestion as one, and a host that lists suggestions over MCP may offer `items` to accept some of a run's changes; a workflow it authors may archive on a schedule and keep its rules as records an owner edits.
- b2afea5: An iPad in Safari: the app bar fits, and the Scene doesn't jump (Roadmap request:top-nav-extends-off-the-screen-horizontally-on-an-ipad-in-safari and request:scene-view-is-glitchy-on-an-ipad-awkwardly-shoots-around-on-clicking-different-things, filed from Homeflow). Three things were found, by following the bar and the city frame by frame in WebKit at each iPad's size.
  
  The bar ran past the screen on an iPad Mini turned (1024 wide): five places stood, and the person sat 21 pixels off the right edge with no page scroll to reach it. The room for the places was measured to Find's end, and Find stops giving at its least, so once the tools after it — the scene's Activity, arriving after the places were weighed — took more than the row had left, Find's end went past the row with them and the room still read as enough. The bar now takes what runs past its row off the places' room (and off the switch's words), so a place folds into More instead, and a tool that arrives after the bar was drawn is watched from when it does. Between 640 and 710 pixels the row with the one control ran over the same way, and the switch now draws its marks alone there.
  
  The Scene shot about from altitude. With the panel beside the map, a city that runs past the window keeps the chosen district in sight by the least move — measured from the city's middle every time, so choosing a district at the right edge slid the city left, the next slid it further, and choosing one in plain sight slid it all the way back. The city now moves from where it stands: a layout hands back how far it moved (`CityFrame.stood`), the next is given it (`cityStood` on `LayoutOptions`, `stood` on `placeCity`) at the same cell, and a district already in sight is chosen with nothing moving.
  
  And a window grown by Safari's toolbar folding away, its width the same, laid the scene out again for the taller box and slid the city by half of it. On a touch screen the scene keeps the box it was laid out for while the box grows by no more than a toolbar (120 px) and by just what the window grew; a box that shrinks, a turn, and growth the page made itself are followed at once. The pointer-and-click doubling a tap was suspected of was looked for and is not there: one tap is one focus and one change of view.
  
  The layout's tests hold the city moving from where it stood (`a-district-in-sight-is-chosen-with-nothing-moving`), react's the box held through a toolbar (`the-scene-holds-still-when-a-toolbar-folds-away`), primitives' the bar folding what runs over (`places-stand-in-the-bar-when-there-is-room`), and the viewport harness holds three new claims in WebKit at the iPad Pro 11, iPad (gen 7) and iPad Mini sizes, upright and turned: `theBarFitsAnIpadInBothOrientations` on the todo example's Shell and an app of ten kinds, `aTapOnTheSceneMovesTheCameraOnce` and `theSceneHoldsStillWhenSafarisToolbarCollapses`. A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front (697 bytes minified, 170 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 697 and 175), raised in `scripts/budget-raises/an-ipad-bar-fits-and-the-scene-holds-still-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. `@graview/layout` gains the optional `LayoutOptions.cityStood`, `CityFrame.stood` and the `CityStood` type; nothing that read a frame before reads it differently. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; Homeflow and every hosted app on an iPad get the bar and the Scene as they are on the next build.
- affa912: The right-click menu says what is under the cursor, Roadmap request:add-copy-link-under-open-in-a-new-tab and request:right-click-context-menu-options-to-be-more-relevant-to-the-item-under-my-cursor. On the routed face a record, a list or a place offered "Open in a new tab" and nothing to paste elsewhere; now "Copy link" stands directly under it, puts the whole address on the clipboard and says "Link copied" quietly at the foot of the window. "Inspect" stands at the foot of every menu, after a rule: a page cannot open the browser's developer tools, so it opens a small sheet of what Graview knows of the thing under the cursor — its kind in words and by its key, the record's id, the field, the place and the picture that drew it (a status board, a view, a lens, by its name), the column it stands in and the link to its page, and, for whoever may change the app (a host's `keptViews` that says so, or an app with no policy), how its kind is declared; keys and ids are written as code. The sheet is fetched the first time somebody asks. Text a person selected is no longer swallowed: a right-click on the selected text keeps the browser's own menu, as before, and a right-click elsewhere while it stays selected opens the menu with "Copy" first, which copies the selection; a finger resting on text no longer opens the menu over it — the phone's own selection handles win — and opens it only on a card, a row, a link, a value, a column or another handle. Safari, and every WebKit on a Mac, selects the word under a right-click before the menu event, so the menu used to find "text somebody selected" under every right-click on words and leave the browser's own; a selection the right-click itself made now counts for nothing — it is put back and the menu opens, while text selected before the press still keeps the browser's menu. And a column under the cursor answers: a status board's column now says `data-column="<field>:<value>"` (with `data-column-kind` and `data-column-label`), a worker view may mark any region the same way, and a right-click on its ground offers "Add a task in Doing" — the kind's add act begun with the column's value given, its form asking only what is left (`PointerMenu.begin`, which the Inspector's menu opens on) — and "Show only Doing", the place or list narrowed by its address (`?filter=status:doing`, as `narrowedPath` writes it). A menu target says the column as `MenuTarget.column` (`MenuColumn`: kind, field, value, label), so a host's section can read it too. The graview-view-links skill says the attribute. `the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor.test.tsx` holds it, and the menu harness proves it at a desk and a phone's width: theMenuOffersCopyLinkUnderOpenInANewTab, inspectSaysWhatIsUnderTheCursor, aSelectionIsCopiedNotSwallowed, and aColumnOffersAddInItAndShowOnlyIt on the tasks' status board and on a worker view that marks its own columns; its long-press claim now rests on a value rather than on a heading's text.
  
  The rows, the column's reading and the sheet's door cost the pages face alone 4.8 KB more (4_939 bytes minified, 2_070 gzipped), every face 10.1 KB (10_307 / 4_261) and the embed with the studio handed in 4.9 KB (5_001 / 1_974), each under a raise naming this change in `scripts/budget-raises/the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor-*.json`; a hosted page stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. `@graview/react` gains the optional `MenuTarget.column`, `PointerMenu.column` and `PointerMenu.begin` and the type `MenuColumn`, all additive. What Graview Cloud does: nothing is required; a view that draws columns marks each `data-column="<field>:<value>"` to have the menu offer adding in it and showing only it, and a host that keeps the declaration says `may.lenses` or `may.views` through `keptViews` for Inspect to show the declaration.

## 0.1.62

### Patch Changes

- 055b1eb: A suggestion takes a vote from someone who may not accept it, Roadmap f-suggestion-votes (the framework half; Cloud's signed-out visitors, its tidying notice count and the owner's switch are Cloud's). Someone who could see a suggestion and could not make its change — a reader, a visitor by link, a seat signed out on a public app — pressed Accept in the Suggested tray and met a refusal. Now their Accept and Decline are a vote, for or against: an op of the built-in `vote-suggestion` act (`VOTE_SUGGESTION`, `{ suggestion, vote: "for" | "against" | null }`), which every store answers to and no list offers, as `keep-link` is — `mutation` the call, no primitives, the vote on the op (`Operation.vote`), the records the suggestion names as what its sentence read, so a seat kept from one is kept from the vote as it is from the suggestion. One standing vote per person, the latest, an agent's counted as the person it acts for; voting again changes it and `null` withdraws it (`voteSuggestion`); a vote is its voter's to undo, the system's, or a seat's the policy grants every act. `acceptSuggestion` and `declineSuggestion` ask the store the question Accept would meet (`mayDecide`: every call of the suggestion permitted to the seat) and, where the answer is no, keep the vote instead and answer `voted`; where it is yes they make or decline it as ever, and a store with no policy lets everyone decide as it always did. Every entry of `suggestions()` carries `tally: { for, against, mine? }` — the reader's own standing vote as `mine` — so a list sorts on it, and `votesIn` and `tallyOf` read it off a log. The tray says "Your vote was recorded" to a voter and keeps the row, the pressed button saying which way they voted, Withdraw vote beside it; to whoever may decide it shows "4 for, 1 against" beside Accept, kept current as votes land. An app may say `"tidying": { "autoAcceptAt": 4 }` at the top of its document (`GraviewApp.tidying`, `StoreOptions.tidying`, handed to the store by every host that hands it the policy): at that many votes for, the app's own actor makes the suggestion in the deciding vote's own gesture — one batch, one undo — with its lineage naming the suggestion and the voters (`lineage.voters`, at most 200), having rehearsed that the actor would be let: a record a person said to leave alone leaves the vote standing and the suggestion waiting. `graview check` warns `tidying-votes-unreachable` when the number is set and the app has no policy, since then nobody votes. The agent tools' `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` answer `voted` from such a seat and `list_suggestions` carries each `tally`; no tool is added and no input schema moves. `a-suggestion-takes-a-vote-from-someone-who-may-not-accept-it.test.ts`, `an-app-says-a-suggestion-is-made-at-so-many-votes.test.ts` and `the-suggested-tray-takes-a-readers-vote-and-shows-the-tally-to-who-may-decide.test.tsx` hold it, and the chrome harness presses Accept as a reader on both faces and reads the tally as the keeper (aSuggestionTakesAVoteFromAReaderAndShowsTheTallyToWhoMayDecide). The graview-tidying skill says it. A hosted page carries 1.5 KB more up front (1_544 bytes minified, 486 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 1.4 KB: 1_470 and 479): the act, the vote's place on an op, the gesture's queue and who may take a vote back — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-suggestion-takes-a-vote-*.json` say each entry's bytes, every one measured against a fresh build of main.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — one new optional op field, `vote`, on the op of one new built-in act, with no primitives and no new primitive; a log holding one folds the same on every build, and a build before this one keeps the field on an op it receives and lists the vote as a change that touched nothing. Stored formats — nothing moves: the op is new and absent in an older store, and a lineage may name `voters`, a key an older build keeps as it keeps any it does not know. The wire — a vote travels as an ordinary `call`; a server before this one refuses the act as unknown, so a host reads `capabilities().version` before it offers a vote to a seat. The declaration — a new optional top-level key, `tidying: { autoAcceptAt }`, refused by nothing that compiled before, and one new warning code, `tidying-votes-unreachable`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` gain `voted` in their answer and `list_suggestions` gains `tally`, both additive.
  
  What Graview Cloud does: on this version its readers' and link visitors' Accept and Decline in the tray become votes with no change of its own, its tidying notice may count a suggestion's `tally`, and its owner's switch is `tidying.autoAcceptAt` in the document rather than a setting of its own; votes from seats it signs out are its half to mint.
- 8f990e3: A video plays as a hero background, Roadmap f-video-hero (the framework half). A record's video was a player with the browser's controls, poster first, and nothing else: an immersive home that wanted a clip running quietly behind its words had no way to ask for one, and a worker view may not draw `<video>` at all. `<graview-video autoplay>` is now a background — muted, looping, inline and without controls, since autoplay without muted never plays and so the one word means all of it — and `<graview-video cover>` fills the box the view sized (`object-fit: cover`; the view's own stylesheet rounds and clips the box). The poster stays the value's, and the source, the poster and the controls stay the host's: `src`, `poster`, `controls`, `loop` and `muted` on a `<graview-video>` are refused as before. Where the person asked for less motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`) a background stands still at its poster, fetching only its metadata, and is let go — or held again — as the setting changes while the page is open. The face's player (`Files` as a field or an image block, `VideoWay`: `autoplay`, `cover`) draws the same, with nothing said under a background. A view is handed `poster: true` among a video's facts where it has one, never the poster's path. A headless run lists each video the view places — `videos` on the place's description, each `{ record, field, text, name, source, duration, poster, autoplay, cover, playable, why }` — and says it in one line under "Videos:" in the place's words, `playable` being a source every browser plays (MP4, WebM or Ogg, what a host's upload transcodes to) with a poster to stand at, so an agent that wrote a hero confirms it plays without opening a browser; the part itself reads "The waves at dusk, a 45-second video, playing as a background, muted and looping, filling its box". The guest's, the primitives' and core's tests hold it (`a-video-plays-as-a-hero-background`, `a-place-description-lists-its-videos`), and the guest harness's pictures run places the porch's clip as a hero on every engine: played by nobody's press, muted and looping and covering its 120 px box, and still at its poster where the page asked for less motion. The guest README and the graview-view-drawing skill say it. The open kit's table and the host's player cost the guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit 1_226 / 377 more, a worker view's runtime 56 / 15 more, every face 2_721 / 939 more and Cloud's worker 3_446 bytes more, each under a raise naming this changeset; nothing a hosted page loads up front changed, and the guest host before a worker is drawn is as it was.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats and the declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. The wire — a worker view's file facts gain `poster: true` on a video that has one; a view that read none is unmoved. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; a place's description (`describePlace`, a headless run) gains the optional `videos` list and its text a "Videos:" section only where a view places one. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for a view that writes `<graview-video autoplay cover>`; its `propose_view` answer carries `videos`, which its authoring guide may point an agent at to confirm a hero plays; an app-level reel not tied to a record stays Cloud's half.
- 55649fe: The four follow-ups to 0.1.56 (Roadmap f-partial-dates-in-conversation, f-partial-dates-arithmetic, f-blocks-home-flagged, f-logo-on-dark-light-ground). A date has known only its year or its month since 0.1.56, and four surfaces had not caught up with it or with the dark page beside it.
  
  A rule compares a date that knows only its year or its month by the span it covers (`daySpanOf` on `@graview/core`: `2006` is January 1 to December 31, `2016-05` the first of May to the thirty-first). It is before another date only when the whole span is, after it only when the whole span is, and equal only when the spans are — so a month the day is in is neither before nor after `today()`, where by their letters alone "2006" < "2006-03-04" had been true. `days()` and `hours()` between such dates are measured from the first day each covers, and `date()` of one is that day. The calendar lens draws a month alone as an all-day bar across its month, and a span that ends in a month runs to that month's last day; a year alone is still not placed — a bar across a whole year says nothing a grid can place, and a day picked for it would be a false exact date — and the lens says once how many it left off ("2 know only their year, so they are not placed."; `daysCovered` and `knowsOnlyItsYear` on `@graview/primitives`). `a-rule-compares-a-partial-date-by-the-span-it-covers.test.ts` and `a-calendar-draws-a-month-alone-across-its-month.test.tsx` hold each case.
  
  The seat's own reading of a sentence keeps a year or a month as said: "Acme was founded in 2006" proposes `founded: "2006"` and "founded in May 2016" (or "sept. 2016", "2016-05", "5/2016") `2016-05`, for a date that may know only that much (`partial` on its form field), where every date had been filled with the day it was; a date that must be a day is still the day it is, and a whole day, an id or a number in the sentence is not read as a year. A record's facts say such a date back as "May 2016" or "2006", never "2016-05". `the-conversation-keeps-a-year-or-a-month-as-said.test.ts` holds it.
  
  A home written as blocks marks each record it draws that a broken rule names, as the derived home's chips do: the kit's flag beside its name, said as implicated in a problem, its own card in the warning tone, and `data-graview-flagged` on the listed item; `SpecPlace` takes `flagged`, and the home's view passes what it is handed. `a-blocks-home-marks-a-record-that-breaks-a-rule.test.tsx` holds it.
  
  A light scheme whose ground is dark has been drawn as a dark page since 0.1.56, but the light logo was still the one drawn there, so a dark mark made for paper vanished on it. `logoScheme(brand, scheme)` on `@graview/core` says which scheme's logo a scheme draws — the dark one in the dark scheme and on a dark light-ground — and `logoFor`, the theme's sheet and a static page's sheet hide the other by it, inverting a light logo that asked to be (`darkFallback`) there as in the dark scheme. `a-light-scheme-drawn-dark-draws-the-dark-logo.test.ts`, the logo case in `the-logo-switches-with-the-scheme.test.tsx` and `a-static-page-on-a-dark-light-ground-draws-the-dark-logo.test.ts` hold it.
  
  The span a date covers, a rule comparing by it, and which scheme's logo a scheme draws cost a hosted page 0.8 KB more up front (857 bytes minified, 316 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.8 KB (835 / 313), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 2_829 / 1_139 more, a visitor's form alone 1_997 / 827, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 840 / 290, every face 1_063 gzipped, the studio handed in 975 gzipped, the guest host 424 / 162 before a worker is drawn, 424 / 156 registering a view, 409 / 150 drawing a worker and 794 / 329 on the open kit, and Cloud's worker 1_214 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_062_625 B (1038 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — no shape or code changes; a rule that compares a date holding a year or a month alone (which a document's date has accepted since 0.1.56) is now judged by the span the value covers rather than by its letters, so such a rule may hold or break where it did the opposite before, and `days()` over one is measured from the span's first day. The JavaScript API — additive: `daySpanOf` and `logoScheme` on `@graview/core`, `daysCovered` and `knowsOnlyItsYear` on `@graview/primitives`, `flagged` on `SpecPlace`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its agents say "founded in 2006" and the seat keeps `2006`, and a black brand with a dark logo shows it on every face.
- 1d2b75a: A notice carries its tone as a chip, not a side accent (FR-236), Roadmap f-notices-without-side-accent; and the framework's own error toasts offer a host's "Report this" (FR-237), Roadmap f-notices-report-hook. Every toast and banner drew a thick colored border on its left side alone, and so did the warn boxes on a record's page, the problems page and a place printed as static HTML — a major AI tell, Nick said. A notice is now one hairline all round in the app's radius, its tone a small tinted chip at its head carrying a glyph (✓, !, i), hidden from a reader, who is told the tone by the status or alert the notice is announced through as before; the warn boxes keep their tint under the same hairline and radius. AA holds in both schemes (the chrome harness measures 15.9 and 15.6 against 4.5). Graview Cloud gives its own error toasts a "Report this" that opens its feedback dialog prefilled; the toasts the framework raises itself — a tidying the host refused, a take-back that failed — could carry none. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `@graview/embed/pages` and the Shell take `onReport(report)` beside `barActions`: given, every warning or error toast the framework raises draws a native button, "Report this", named for the toast ("Report this: “Up next” could not be hidden."), that hands the hook `{ message, code?, place?, record?, version }` — the toast's words, a refusal's code when it had one, the place the reader is on (a list's or a place's slug, `home`, `overview` on the scene) and the record when one is open, and `FRAMEWORK_VERSION`. The toast stays while the button has focus (the stack is held, as under a pointer) and stays once pressed, so the words are there to read beside the host's dialog. What the framework says is told apart from what the host says by the board: the app's own surfaces speak on `frameworkVoice(board)`, which marks each notice `from: "framework"`, and a host's `notify` never does, so the host's own notices carry no such button. `Notice` gains `code` and `from`; `NoticeReport` and `NoticeReporting` are the shapes; `sceneReportWhere` says where the scene's reader is. The primitives' tests (`a-notice-carries-its-tone-as-a-chip-not-a-side-accent`, `an-error-toast-offers-the-hosts-report-this`), the embed's (`a-hosts-report-this-answers-the-frameworks-own-toast`) and the pages' broken-rule box hold it, and the chrome harness holds `noticesCarryNoSideAccent` (no element in a toast or a banner has a border on its left wider than its other sides, in both schemes) and `anErrorToastOffersTheHostsReport` (a host that refuses a change to the declaration, Hide pressed on a list: the button named for the toast, the toast held while it has focus, the report handed with the list's slug and the version, the toast gone once focus has moved on; without the hook, no button). The host-page skill and the embed's README say it beside `notify`, and `docs/stability.md` says what a harness reaches. A hosted page carries 0.8 KB more up front (780 bytes minified, 320 gzipped; handed a compiled app, the same 0.8 KB: 780 and 320) — the framework's voice, the scene's where and the frame's hand of `onReport`; the chip, the button and the version it hands ride the notices' door — under raises named `notices-without-the-side-accent-and-a-hosts-report-this-*` in `scripts/budget-raises/`, with the pages face alone 460 / 160 and every face 40 gzipped; the scene face before it draws measured 1_062_059 B (1038 KB). Both are said as shipped: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-236 and FR-237.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-236 and FR-237. The look (not a surface): a notice's side accent is gone, its chip is `[data-testid="notice-chip"]` and its sentence `[data-testid="notice-sentence"]`, and a reportable toast carries `[data-testid="notice-report"]`. What Graview Cloud does: hand `mount` its feedback dialog as `onReport`, prefilled from the report, and keep its own toasts' "Report this" as they are.
- 0e74201: Ask AI in an app proposes a change to the app itself (Roadmap f-seat-reshape). The seat's tools read, and the right-click menu's suggestions — learned and tidying — cover records, so "we also need to track who's driving" or "split this into before and after the trip" had no path inside the app: it went to a chat somewhere else. Now the person's words are matched to structure through the same judgments as the new-app starter: `@graview/tools/reshape`, a new entry, asks a Noul per trait the app lacks (`need:<trait>`: "does what they asked for need people who own things, which this app has no part for yet?") and a Noul per part it has (`drop:kind:<name>`, `drop:trait:<name>`: "did they ask to stop keeping areas?") over one state — what they said and the app in words — through whatever `decide` the host passes (Jev, as `dream` takes one; a stub in a test); `readReshape(answers, thresholds)` reads the answers (a trait added at 0.7, a kind or trait dropped at 0.7 — dropping loses records, so the bar is never lower) with every raw Noul kept; `reshapeCard({ said, document, add, drop })` says the card in words — `said` in one sentence ("This adds people who own things and keeps categories and vendors."), `adds` (each trait with what its module brings, from a new `TRAIT_BRINGS` beside `TRAIT_WORDS`: "a person kind (or the people the app has), an owner link, Give it to someone, …"), `keeps`, `drops`, and `breaking` as far as the seat can say ("All categories records go, with their links.") — and `proposeReshape({ said, document, decide })` runs the turn, its proposal null when the reading changes nothing. The conversation draws the card beneath the seat's answer (`ChatPanel`): added, kept and dropped as chips the person turns off and on, each toggle said again by the same function so what they accept is always the reading the card shows, breaking changes above the press; Accept hands the proposal — `{ said, add, drop, card }`, the reading as the person left it, never edits — to the host's `onProposeChange(proposal) → "applied" | "declined"`, on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` (so `mount`, `<Embed>` and the pages alone take it) and the pages' context, beside `onKeepLens`. The host composes the edits from that reading: `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` on `@graview/core/compose` runs `startingDocument` on the app's own document and answers its `edits` (which `startingDocument` now carries beside `took`, `left` and `said`: the ones that took, in order, replaying onto the base in one `editDocument` call to the document it composed) with `breaking` said from each removal (`breakingOf`) and `notDone`; the host previews them with its data and applies them as the person, as one undoable change, under its own policy, or declines. Code writes every edit, the model never a kind, a field or a rule — and the composer never rides on a page: a page that fetched `@graview/core/compose` when asked made the editor and the critic live up front, which no page carries, so the vocabulary and a document's reading alone stand on a new entry, `@graview/core/traits` (`TRAITS`, `TRAIT_WORDS`, `TRAIT_BRINGS`, `traitsOfDocument`, `hasTrait`, `traitWords`), `traitsOf` of a TypeScript app stays on `/compose` with the composer, and the document barrel says `documentOf` and `rememberDocument` from where they live rather than through the writer. A host's model with tools is offered `propose_change` on its turn — the Roadmap's `propose-change`, spelled as the surface spells its tools — told the card and never an edit, and the card it proposed travels on the reply. The tool exists only for a seat whose role may change the declaration (`mayChangeDeclaration` on `@graview/tools`, the judgment the studio's door now shares: an owner or editor, never a viewer) with a host that handed the hook and a model that decides, over an app with a document to change — the one it was compiled from (`documentOfApp`); an app declared in code remembers itself first (`rememberDocument(app, toDocument(app).document)`, as Things does for its harness) or is offered none. For any other seat there is no door and no card, nothing refused. The panel reads the words for a change to the app conservatively ("we also need", "keep track of who", "add a field", "no longer need … any more"), and a reading that adds and drops nothing goes on to the usual answer. `graview serve`'s own seat passes no hook: the served page has no path that writes its document. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (145 bytes minified, 70 gzipped: the provider and the frame carry the hook, and the barrel exports `documentOf`); handed a compiled app, 0.2 KB (197 and 197); the pages face alone 334 / 218 more, the embed with the studio handed in 702 / 950, every face 23_150 / 9_033 across the doors a face can fetch — the reading, the card and the seat's tool riding the seat's model, never up front — and the guest host registering a worker view 0 / 1, its own bytes unchanged and the chunk names its imports carry gzipping a byte larger beside the new doors, each under a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-*.json` naming this changeset. `the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-from-words.test.ts`, `the-seat-offers-a-change-to-the-app-only-with-a-host-to-apply-it.test.tsx` and the starting-document test hold it, and the seat harness drives the keeper's "we also need who's driving" on Things to the card and through Accept, with a member drawing none. The agent-seat and studio skills say it.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged; `startingDocument` answers one more key, `edits`, and `composeReshape`, `breakingOf` and `TRAIT_BRINGS` are additions on `/compose`, `@graview/core/traits` a new entry carrying what `/compose` already named. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged on `graview mcp` and `toolDefinitions`: `propose_change` is listed only on a seat's turn whose context carries `reshape`, which only a host with `onProposeChange` hands it. What Graview Cloud does: pass `onProposeChange` to `mount` for an owner's or editor's seat; on its server, `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` from the proposal, preview the edits through its own propose_change and apply them through apply_change as the person; decline for a plan without structure changes.

## 0.1.61

### Patch Changes

- c9fd45a: A choice takes several of its options at once (FR-233), Roadmap f-multi-select-enum; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-233. An enum took one option and a list took free words, so a memo's recipients — the CTO, the developers, QA, product — had nowhere to be several fixed options. A field is now `{ "type": "enum", "multiple": true, "options": [...] }`: the one shape an enum already has, so its `options`, its `optionLabels` (0.1.56) and `set-options` carry over unchanged, rather than a `list` with `options`, which would have given options a second home on a type whose `of` already says what it holds. It compiles to a list of the options (`z.array(z.enum(...))`), which `fieldSpecOf` reads back as the same field and the generated types say as an array of the union. A record's facts, a card, a glance, a change's sentence and a sheet (`/csv`, `/words`) say each option it holds by its label, "CTO, QA"; a form on the routed face asks with a box per option and sends the list of those ticked, in the options' order; a record's page changes it in place as the same boxes; the scene's ask strip already toggled several of a choice. A list filters by one of its options (`/arrange`, the filter chips and a view's `where`) and is neither sorted nor grouped by it — a record would stand in several groups — which `graview check` says of a list declared grouped by one and of a board bound to one. An import (`/import`, `import_rows`) reads a cell that lists them, "CTO; QA" or "developers, product"; a Notion connection holds it in a multi-select (`notionFieldType`, `several choices` in `NOTION_FITS`), never a select; a derived act's JSON Schema and tool schema ask for an array of the enum (`/json-schema`); a migration keeps the options that fit when a field changes between one and several (`coerce`). The studio's meta-schema has `multiple` on a field, `add-field` and `retype-field` take it, `set-multiple` changes it alone, and a declaration's list of an enum round-trips through the studio's graph, its source and its edits. `multiple` on a field that is not an enum is refused as a shape finding at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, in `graview check` and in `document.schema.json` alike; a default outside the options, or a single one where a list is wanted, is `default-option`. The graview-node-kind skill says the shape in one line. `a-choice-takes-several-options.test.ts`, `a-form-asks-for-several-options-as-boxes.test.tsx`, `several-options-changed-in-place-as-boxes.test.tsx` and `a-choice-of-several-round-trips-through-the-studio.test.ts` hold it, and the field-types harness opens a rehearsal's sections on the page and in the form.
  
  Reading the shape, compiling the list and saying its options costs a hosted page 0.5 KB more up front (522 bytes minified, 278 fewer gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.2 KB (161 more minified, 271 fewer gzipped), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 2_246 / 565 more, a visitor's form alone 709 / 126, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 863 / 102, every face 1_480 gzipped, the guest host 36 / 12 registering a view, 6 drawing a worker and 72 on the open kit, and Cloud's worker 4_641 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_054_746 B (1031 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a record of such a field holds a JSON array of its options, as a list field does. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a field gains the key `multiple` (an enum's only; on any other type it is refused as `shape` at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, as `options` is), `default-option` also judges a list default, `retype-field` takes `multiple`, and a Notion `connection-field-type` finding names `several choices` for such a field; every other code is unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — a form field of a list of an enum is now one `choice` control with `multiple: true` where it was a `list` of `choice` controls (`FormField`); a tool's input schema for the argument is the array of the enum it already was. What Graview Cloud does: declares `"multiple": true` on an enum that takes several; draws a `choice` form field with `multiple` as checkboxes if it draws its own forms.
- fddebfd: A record's card on the Scene says there is more where it is cut (FR-231), Roadmap f-scene-preview-card-scrolls. A worker view's card is held to its box and never scrolls — a page inside a picture was what FR-141 and f-scene-record-view-card took away — and said so only by fading its foot, with Open at its head: a person reading a long race card scrolled, nothing moved, and nothing said why. Of the three shapes the ask allowed, the card keeps its own: it still neither scrolls nor grows, and now says so where the eye is. Where there is more than the card holds, the fade is taller and ends in the card's own panel color, and on that ground at the foot stands a visible More, named for the card ("More of Write the brief"), after the view in the tab order and pressed without choosing the card: a link to the record's page or the place where there are pages — the page's address where the host's page has one, a link by its role and Enter where it has none — and, in a Shell with no pages, a press that grows the card in place as Expand does. A card that holds all it draws says nothing, and an expanded one says nothing until Collapse. Nothing of the camera changes: a wheel over the card is the browser's, as before, and no card scrolls. `react`'s tests hold it (`a-cut-scene-card-says-there-is-more.test.tsx`), and `verify-address` presses More on the Scene at 1440 and 390 and finds it at the foot on the fade, named, landing on the place and on the record's page, and a button in the Shell that is gone once the card is grown (aCutSceneCardSaysMoreAtItsFootAndMoreLandsWhereOpenDoes). `graview-worker-view` says the card is faded with More at its foot. FR-231 is said as shipped: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-231.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a host that drew a Scroll hint of its own over the card can take it away.
- 5ec4dcf: A host's buttons stand on the app bar (FR-227), Roadmap f-host-buttons-in-the-app-bar. Graview Cloud's "Send feedback" could sit only in the person's menu (`hostActions`) or the right-click menu, two taps from a reader, and a host may float nothing over an app (FR-72). An embed, the pages alone and the Shell now take `barActions: [{ label, icon?, onSelect }]`: each is a button on the bar itself, on both faces, before the standing and the person, named by its label with its mark (an emoji, a glyph or an inline `<svg>`) hidden from a reader who cannot see it. Where the bar has their width over a phone's they stand on the row; where it does not — a phone, a small box — they fold into one control, "More", that lists them, one of the bar's own family of popovers. `handle.setBarActions` changes them live, as `setHostActions` does the menu's; `undefined` takes them off, and nothing is drawn when a host gives none. They are the host's only: no declaration and no sandboxed view can put a button on the bar. The primitives' and the embed's tests hold it (`a-hosts-buttons-stand-in-the-bar-or-fold`, `a-hosts-buttons-are-in-the-app-bar-on-both-faces`), and the chrome harness presses "Send feedback" by the keyboard on both faces, standing on a desk and folded on a phone. The host-page skill and the embed's README say it beside `hostActions`. A hosted page carries 3.2 KB more up front (3_262 bytes minified, 128 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 3_260 and 250): the bar's buttons, the control they fold into and its popover, and `barActions` on the frame and both handles — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-hosts-buttons-stand-on-the-app-bar-*.json` say each entry's bytes, the PDF's one line (FR-234, the same pull request) riding the files part behind its door. FR-227 is said as shipped: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-227.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-227. What Graview Cloud does: hand `mount` its "Send feedback" as `barActions`, and keep `hostActions` for the menu.
- 166cf1d: A PDF that cannot be shown is one line (FR-234), Roadmap f-pdf-fallback-one-line. A record's PDF was an `<object>` 420 px tall with a frame, its fallback sentence inside it: where a browser draws no PDF inline — an iPhone, or a host whose page says `object-src 'none'` — the reader got a tall empty box with one sentence. The file's line now stands outside the box and says it all, "week44.pdf · PDF · 1.2 MB — Open / Download" (Open in a tab, Download with the file's name), and the box takes its height and its frame only once the browser has loaded the PDF into it (a browser with a PDF viewer — Chrome, headed Chromium — fires `load` on the object; Playwright's headless engines, an iPhone and a page whose CSP refuses the route fire nothing, and the box stays at nothing). The host contract says the rest: a host's page that carries a `Content-Security-Policy` allows its files route in `object-src` if it wants a PDF drawn inline (`docs/stability.md`, the ship and host-page skills). A primitives test holds it (`a-pdf-that-cannot-be-shown-is-one-line`), and the files harness reads the line on every engine and holds the box to its state — sized once loaded, nothing until then. FR-234 is said as shipped: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-234.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-234. What Graview Cloud does: allow its files route in the hosted page's `object-src`, or nothing — the line is there either way.
- cab3b1d: A suggestion's record chips open their record (FR-225), Roadmap f-suggestion-chips-open-their-record. The Suggested tray named each record a suggestion was about as a chip with a pick target only the scene's own pointer handler answered, so on either face and from the keyboard it looked pressable and did nothing. Each is now the button a record is in Activity (`NamedRecord`, shared by both): on the scene it focuses and chooses the record, on the routed face it opens the record's page, and the tray closes behind the reader; the record's name is its accessible name and "Go to …" its title, and nothing in the tray carries a pick target any more. Primitives' and pages' tests hold it (`a-suggestion-chip-opens-its-record.test.tsx`, pressed on the scene and on a face that says where a record opens, axe-clean; `a-suggestion-chip-opens-the-records-page.test.tsx`). FR-225 is said as shipped.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-225. The look: the tray's chips are buttons carrying `data-testid="suggestion-record"` and `data-record`, as Activity's `activity-record` do. What Graview Cloud does: nothing.
- 08d4b4c: Activity never scrolls sideways (FR-226), Roadmap f-activity-panel-no-sideways-scroll. A change to a record named in 120 characters with no space ran its sentence past the panel's right edge, and the row's "as it was" and undo buttons sat past it, reached only by scrolling sideways — the same fault the Suggested tray had in 0.1.60. The panel is now no wider than the page (`min(300px, 100vw − 16px)`) and hides what would run sideways; every row — a person's change, the agent's calls, the actor's digest and each change in it, a withheld change and the AI's entries — is one column that may shrink (`min-width: 0`, `minmax(0, 1fr)`), its header wraps, its sentence takes the line and breaks anywhere (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), so the buttons beside it stay beside it, its chip row wraps, and a record's chip is never wider than its row, its whole name in its title. Primitives' test holds what the panel is drawn with (`activity-never-scrolls-sideways.test.tsx`), and `pnpm verify chrome` measures it at 320 px on both faces (`activityNeverScrollsSideways`): the panel, every row, the long row's buttons and its chip inside the panel, and the panel inside the page. FR-226 is said as shipped.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-226. The look: Activity's rows carry `data-graview-activity-head` on their header and `data-graview-activity-words` on their sentence. What Graview Cloud does: nothing.

## 0.1.60

### Patch Changes

- 3e2d346: Typing into a field rides a TypeScript act that writes it only where that act would run on the record. A keystroke (`type-text`) rode an act only while its declared rule (`allowedWhen`, `allowedOn`) held, but a TypeScript act whose rule lives in its `apply` — it throws `ActRefusal` for a published note — said nothing against it, so a seat refused "Revise" on a published note typed the body over all the same. Such an act is now asked: it is run on the record with the field's own value, against a context that writes nothing, and a refusal by its own rule (an `ActRefusal` whose reason is not `invalid`) keeps typing out; an `invalid` one ("nothing to change"), or an argument the record does not hold, does not. An act whose rule reads an argument a keystroke never hands it says its rule about the record as `allowedOn`, which is believed and the act not run. The same question (`wouldRun`, on `@graview/core`) decides what a record offers to change in place: `editableFields` leaves out a field whose act would refuse the record now, and a field typed together offers Edit only where the store would take a keystroke, so a published note shows no Edit; the live editor asks again before each keystroke and is read only, saying so, where a lock lands while it is open. `graview check` warns `typing-act-without-allowed-on` where a TypeScript act that writes a field typed together needs an argument a keystroke never gives it and declares no `allowedOn`, since a rule that reads that argument is never asked. `a-keystroke-rides-a-typescript-act-only-where-it-would-run.test.ts`, `an-edit-is-not-offered-on-a-record-its-act-refuses.test.ts` and `check-says-a-typing-act-it-cannot-ask.test.ts` hold it. A hosted page carries 0.6 KB more up front, handed a compiled app or not: `wouldRun`, which the fields offered to edit ask.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. Check finding codes — one new warning, `typing-act-without-allowed-on`, judging only a TypeScript act's `allowedOn`; no code changes meaning and no new error. A seat that typed into a record through a TypeScript act whose `apply` refuses that record is now refused the keystroke, as it was refused the act, and is offered no Edit there.
