## 0.1.59

### Patch Changes

- 5bf24ba: A document's typeface is a list of names quoted in pairs, and an embed's scoped theme keeps every rule in its box even when it is not. A face with a quote it never closed — `"display": "Georgia'"` — passed `graview check` (the check took the quote off before it looked the name up) and the page's brand (which refused only `url(`, `@import` and `;{}<>\`), and was written into the embed's style sheet. CSS ends a string at the end of its line, but `withinTheBox`, which puts every rule of an embed's sheet under its element, read the string on to the next matching quote, so it missed where the block ended and left the rules after it as they were: 43 selectors (`h1`–`h4`, `code`, `kbd`, `samp`, the button states and many `.graview-*` rules) restyled the host page outside the embed, through the whole embed, the pages embed and a visitor's form alike. Now one rule judges a face in the checker and in `brandOf`: each family is a name with no quote in it, or one in a single matching pair with none inside, and a face with a line break, a control character or `/*` is refused, so `"Times New Roman"` and `'Source Serif 4'` are taken and written as given while `Georgia'` is refused (`brand-font`: "Georgia' is not quoted in one pair") and never written. And `withinTheBox` ends a string at a line break as CSS does, so a face that reached it some other way still cannot carry the rules after it out of the box. `the-whole-brand-is-in-the-document.test.ts` holds seven unbalanced faces refused and unwritten and four quoted ones taken; `a-scoped-theme-styles-nothing-outside-its-box.test.ts` reads the sheet as CSS does and finds every rule in the box for brands with an open quote in a face. The one rule rides with the brand a page compiles, so a hosted page carries 0.4 KB more up front (433 bytes minified, 223 gzipped), and one handed a compiled app 0.4 KB (433, 161), under raises naming this changeset.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — no new code: `brand-font` is also said of a face with a quote not closed in its pair, a line break, a control character or `/*`, which compiled before and is refused now; a page compiled without the check leaves such a face out, as it leaves out one that loads.
- 350fc01: A kind's figure is judged as the page reads it, and judged again where it is drawn. `figureFaults` found attributes by looking for a space or a quote before each name, but the HTML parser also takes a `/` as the gap between two attributes, so `<path d="…"/onclick="…"/>` passed `graview check`, the checked `compileDocument` and a model's `drawFigure` as a plain path, and `KindFigure` drew it with a live click handler (a smuggled `style` could stretch it over the page). Now `figureFaults` first reads the art with `svgProblem`, the reader a logo is judged by, which walks tags as the parser does and refuses a handler, a style sheet or anything that reaches outside the picture ("it cannot be drawn as it is: it has an event handler (an on… attribute)"), and its own scan of attribute names counts a `/` as a gap. `figureSvg` hands back inline art only when `svgProblem` finds nothing in it that could run or reach outside it, so `KindFigure` and the studio's proposed drawing draw nothing that could run even from a figure nothing checked — a brand's own drawing, a document compiled without the check. A figure that only breaks the house style (a literal color) still draws. Every shipped figure passes. `a-figure-is-judged-as-the-page-reads-it.test.ts` holds three slashed payloads faulted, undrawn and refused by `compileDocument`; `a-figure-that-could-act-is-not-drawn.test.tsx` holds that `KindFigure` draws no handler from a kind's figure or a brand's. A visitor's form draws figures and did not carry `svgProblem`, so it grows about 4.3 KB; with the other fixes of this batch (a typeface quoted in pairs, a plan review applying only the rows kept) a visitor's form carries 4_368 / 1_818 bytes more (4_739 / 1_970 handed a compiled app), the pages face 104 / 28, every face 948 / 455, Cloud's worker 835 and the scene before it draws 470, each under a raise of its own (`scripts/budget-raises/data-is-never-code-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — no new code: `figure` is said of art with an attribute written after a `/`, which compiled before and is refused now.
- e3566ea: What a page offers to take back is what the store would take back. Activity's "undo" asked only the log (`canUndo`), and the corner's take-back control judged a change no act made as anybody's, so once undo came to judge a change no act made (an import, a sync, the app's own actor), a seat was offered an undo the store then refused — the watch's "offered then refused", on Activity at `?tidy=1`. `store.mayUndo(batches, principal)` now answers with the same judgment `undo` makes, log and policy both; Activity's row and the take-back control offer only what it allows, and `undo` itself refuses through it, so the two cannot drift. Core's undo test holds that every `mayUndo` answer agrees with what `undo` then does. A change that changed nothing — a suggestion, which a person declines by taking it back — is anybody's who sees it to take back, so declining the app's own actor's suggestion is not refused. A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front.
  
  Compatibility: additive — `Store.mayUndo` is new; an undo control no longer appears on a change the seat may not take back.
- 5301cbe: A plan's review applies only the rows a person kept, and a plan's name means only what the plan made. `PlanReview` filed a call on a standing record under a row the plan was making whenever the plan gave the new thing that record's id as its name (`as: "b_herbs"`), so the review read "New Tomatoes" while the calls under it renamed and dug up Herb Bed; declining the row drew it struck through and still ran them, and Apply stayed pressable under "0 of 1 to run". A call waiting on one the seat may not make stayed ready, and `applyPlan` handed a `{ $plan }` name that made nothing on as the literal id, so it acted on whatever record had it. Now a plan's names are its own — `planFrom` refuses a name that is already a record's id, and refuses a call that waits for one it cannot make ("Waits for "seedlings", which this plan cannot make.") — and `applyPlan` stops and takes back what ran when a name it needs made nothing ("Refers to "fresh", which nothing earlier in this plan made."). In the review, a call on a standing record is its own row, named by that record; the press runs only the calls of rows drawn live, planned again so nothing runs without what it waits for, and is not offered when no row is live. `a-plan-name-means-only-what-the-plan-made.test.ts` holds the name refused, the dependent refused, the stop and the undo; `apply-runs-only-the-rows-a-person-kept.test.tsx` holds the standing record named in its own row, a declined row and a refused one running nothing, and a kept row beside a declined one running alone.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. A plan whose name is a record's id, or whose call waits on a refused one, is refused where it ran before.
- d1d4e11: Starting fresh is asked for in the tab that does it, never by a link from somewhere else. Every app `graview create` writes opens its store with `fresh: browserStartsFresh()`, which read `?fresh=1` in the address as asking — so any site, or a message, that sent a person to `https://<app>/?fresh=1` erased their records, their history and their undo with nothing to take back, and `forgetFreshParam` then tidied the flag away. Now the Start fresh controls (the workbench's and the routed face's, and the seedbed's "Load the example garden") keep a one-time value in this tab's `sessionStorage` and go to `?fresh=<that value>` (`freshAddress` and `FRESH_KEY` on `@graview/core`; `freshHref` in `@graview/ship/browser` makes the same address), and `browserStartsFresh` honors the address only when it carries the value the tab kept, forgetting it as it reads it. A `?fresh=1` from elsewhere, a guessed value, or the same address loaded twice discards nothing, and `forgetFreshParam` drops `fresh` from the address whatever it says. A driven browser is unchanged: it starts fresh unless it says `?remember=1`, and takes `?fresh=1` as asking, so every harness and `graview create`'s scaffold work as they did. Ship's browser-adapter test holds that a store opened under `?fresh=1`, `?utm=x&fresh=1&remember=1` or a value the tab did not keep still holds what a person made, and that the control's own address starts fresh once. The graview-ship and graview-seed skills say Start fresh rather than `?fresh=1`.
  
  The one-time value costs a hosted page 0.3 KB more up front (286 bytes minified, 189 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.3 KB (286, 117), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 239 / 96, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 286 / 133, every face 175 / 123 and the guest host registering a worker view 4 gzipped, each under a raise of its own, and the scene before it draws 230 bytes more, under 1023 KB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged: what the browser adapter keeps is read and written as before; only what asks for it to be discarded changed. A host whose own control navigates to `?fresh=1` in a person's browser goes through `freshAddress` (or `freshHref`) now, or the address is not honored.

## 0.1.58

### Patch Changes

- 942e8c9: Find's list stays still under the pointer, and a record's acts are asked for rather than sprung. A record the pointer passed over unfolded its acts under it, so every row below jumped while the pointer was heading for one of them, and the next record it crossed unfolded too. Now passing over a row only lights it, and only when the pointer itself moved: a list that scrolls or draws again under a still pointer lights nothing. Nothing is put into the list, taken out of it or moved. On a desk the lit row is said whole in a panel beside the list — what it is, its name, why the words found it — with its acts there as buttons, a moment behind a pointer crossing rows on its way to them. In a narrow strip or a phone's sheet a record's acts open under its row only when its "⋯" is pressed. From the keyboard → reaches the acts in both, ← comes back, and Enter on an act opens it in the menu on its record as before. A row is a line of its name (two at most, so a long name still shows the word that found it) and a line of why, each cut short with an ellipsis and said whole when the pointer rests on it; the words that matched are marked in the app's accent; an act says only what it does. Each kind's heading says how many the words found of it, and where several kinds share the strip each shows its best five with a row, "All 170 talks matching “data”", that goes into the kind narrowed by the words, as the district's "N match" does. A desk's strip says its keys under the list: ↑↓ move, ↵ open, → actions, esc close. Primitives' tests hold that lighting a record puts in and moves no row, that a mouse event without movement lights nothing, that → and the "⋯" reach the acts, that Enter on an act opens its menu, that the group cap and its "All" row go in narrowed, and that the matched words are marked; verify-navigation adds `theListStaysStillUnderThePointer`, `actsOpenOnlyWhenAskedFor` and `theStripTeachesItsKeys`. A hosted page carries nothing more up front; the scene before it draws carries 10.3 KB more (10_558 bytes minified, under 1021 KB), and every face 9_935 more (3_017 gzipped), under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/find-stays-still-and-acts-are-asked-for-every-face.json`).

## 0.1.57

### Patch Changes

- 2173a1e: A date emptied where it is shown is cleared (Roadmap f-clear-date-in-place). Since 0.1.56 `null` clears a field a record may be without on a derived edit, but the page's in-place editor sent nothing when a person emptied a date, so a wrong date could be changed and never removed. Now emptying a field in place — a date first, and any field that can be emptied: words, a number, an amount — sends the edit `null` where the act clears that field, and the record has none, one change that can be undone; an emptied number is no longer read as zero. A field the record cannot be without keeps its value and says so beside it: `Name can't be empty; it keeps APT1.` An editable field from `@graview/tools/edit` says which with `clearable`: true where its act takes `null` for that field (a derived edit's `clearable`). Primitives' test empties a date in place and the record then has none, empties a text and a number the same way, and finds a required name kept with its sentence.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged on every surface. `EditableField` gains `clearable`, an addition.
- 67e4b0d: A text block says how many lines a card or a row shows of it (FR-218), Roadmap f-card-text-clamp. A card cuts a text block at three lines and a row at one, so `{"text": "{awards}", "tone": "accent"}` on a footballer's card stopped at the third award at 390 pixels and nothing showed the rest in place. A text block may now say `"lines": 6` — that many lines before the text is cut short — or `"lines": 0` to show it whole, on whichever slot draws it; said nowhere, the card keeps its three and the row its one. `graview check` refuses any other value at the block's path (`view-lines`: `lines is 13, and a text block's lines is a whole number from 1 to 12, or 0 to show it whole`), refuses `lines` on any block but text (`unknown-key`), and no longer warns `view-whole-text` of a long text a block measures on purpose. `@graview/core/document.schema.json` says it. The graview-view-specs skill says so. A hosted page carries 0.4 KB more up front (409 bytes minified, 169 gzipped), the pages face alone 555 more (174) and every face 952 (375), each under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-text-block-says-how-many-lines-it-shows-*.json`), and the scene before it draws 955 more, under 1009 KB. Primitives' test draws a card's text with `lines: 6` and with `lines: 0` and finds each said on the element, and the default when it says nothing, and the check takes 0 to 12 and refuses -1, 2.5, 13, "6", true and null. A "more" control that opens the rest in place is not part of this: a card is a link to its record, whose page says the text whole.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: a text block's `lines`, and the finding `view-lines`; a build that predates it refuses `lines` as a key a text block does not say. The wire — additive: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-218. What Graview Cloud does: lets an agent's view spec say `lines` on a text block once `capabilities().shipped` has FR-218.

## 0.1.56

### Patch Changes

- c7bdc23: A date may know only its year or its month, and a wrong date can be taken away (Roadmap f-partial-dates). History often knows a year alone — APT1 was first seen in 2006 — and an agent that could keep only a day stored `2006-01-01`, a false exact date. A document's `date` now holds a year (`2006`) or a month (`2016-05`) as well as a day: said "2006" and "May 2016" wherever a day is said (a record's facts, a card's glance, a template, a change's sentence, `dayAsRead` and `whenAsRead`), sorted as written so a year comes before the months and days within it and a month before its days, and grouped by year or month in the year or month it names (a finer bucket gives it a group of its own, before the days within it). The date's editor on both faces reads "2006", "May 2016", "5/2016" and "2016-05" as a year or a month alone and says it back ("May 2016 (a month alone)"), and an import reads such a cell into a date column. A TypeScript date field (`ISO_DAY`) is unchanged; a document's date field's pattern is `DATE_VALUE` (`^\d{4}(?:-(?:0[1-9]|1[0-2])(?:-\d{2})?)?$`), which also refuses a month 13 it used to take, and its argument shape says `partial: true`.
  
  Clearing: `edit-<kind>` refused `null` ("expected string") and `""` for a date, and `importRows` read an empty cell as no change, so a wrong date could not be removed. Now `null` clears any field a record may be without (declared optional, not required and with no default) on a derived edit — the act says which in `clearable`, and `null` reaches `apply` past the field's own parse, so its tool schema is the field's as before; a required field still refuses it. `importRows(…, { match, blanksClear: true })` clears such a field where a found record's row leaves its cell blank ("First seen: Jan 1, 2006 → nothing"), through the kind's edit act. Core's tests hold a year and a month kept, said, sorted, grouped and read from a sheet, a wrong month refused, `null` clearing and undone, a required field refusing it, and an import that clears only when it says so; a pages test types "May 2016" into an act's form and "2006-02" over a year on the record. A hosted page carries 1.9 KB more up front, and one handed a compiled app 1.7 KB more, under raises of their budgets with Roadmap f-option-labels (`scripts/budget-raises/a-date-may-know-only-its-year-or-its-month-*.json`); the pages face alone and a visitor's form are raised alike, and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 1007 KB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats and the wire — unchanged; a record that holds a year or a month alone is an ordinary string. The declaration — unchanged in shape; a document's date accepts a year or a month alone and refuses a month past 12. Derived tool names and input schemas — a document's date says the pattern above (announced on the conformance fixture `document:vendors`), and a derived edit accepts `null` for a field it may clear. The JavaScript API — additive: `DATE_VALUE` and `PARTIAL_DAY` on `@graview/core`, `readPartialDay` on `@graview/core/words`, `ImportOptions.blanksClear`, `MutationDefinitionSpec.clearable`, `partial` on a date's `ArgShape` and `FormField`, and `partial` on `WhenInput`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its agents keep `2006` rather than `2006-01-01` and clear a wrong date with `null`, and its `import_rows` passes `blanksClear` when the person asks that blank cells clear.
- 846963c: Activity names each merge, removal and archive in a batch, whatever the batch calls itself (Roadmap f-activity-names-merges). A batch that carried its own intent — an agent's "Cursor: merge-actor, edit-threat" — was one row in those words alone, so a count that dropped (software 79 → 78) was never explained. The row now says the batch's intent and then, by each op's own sentence, every op that takes a record out of the counts: "Cursor: merge-actor, edit-threat — Merge APT-1 into APT1; Remove Comment Crew; Archive Sandworm". An op that changes a record and takes nothing out is still said by the batch's words; an undo says what it took back in its own; an op the seat may not see is not said. Both faces' Activity read it, and so does each change inside a digest.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.
- 92040e8: The Suggested tray never scrolls sideways (Roadmap f-suggested-tray-overflow). With seventeen suggestions in Threat Watch, each one's sentence ("Merge @tanstack/react-router into @tanstack/* (42 packages)"), its "based on …" line and its record chips ran past the panel's right edge, and the panel scrolled sideways. The panel is now no wider than the page (`min(320px, 100vw − 16px)`) and hides what would run sideways; its rows and their lines may shrink (`min-width: 0`, one `minmax(0, 1fr)` column), the sentence, the basis and a refusal break anywhere (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), the chip row and the Accept, Decline and Not now row wrap, and a chip still too wide is cut with an ellipsis, its whole name in its title. `pnpm verify chrome` holds it at 320 px on both faces with a record named in 120 characters and no space (`theSuggestedTrayNeverScrollsSideways`): before, the panel held 932 px of content in 318.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The look: the tray's lines carry `data-graview-suggestion-words` and its chip row `data-graview-suggestion-records`.

## 0.1.55

### Patch Changes

- 3648ead: An app says who it is for and what they come to do (Roadmap f-app-jobs). Nothing in a declaration said whom it served or what they come to it to find out, so every look was assembled field by field from a type table and a model was told the kinds and the acts and nothing about the people. A document takes `purpose` now, and `defineApp` the same shape: `for`, a sentence ("The couple and their planner, mostly on phones"), and up to twelve `jobs`, each a question in the people's words (`ask`) answered by the records of one `kind` a rule-language condition holds for (`where`, the record as its subject, read in a TypeScript app exactly as in a document), in an order (`sort`, a field, a computed field or `label`, or `{ by, direction }`), with the act people usually take next on one (`act`), for some `roles` or everybody. `graview check` holds each to the declaration, in a document at `purpose.jobs.<n>.<key>` and in a TypeScript app at `defineApp(…).purpose.jobs[<n>]`: a kind the app does not have (`job-kind`), a `where` that does not read or names what the kind does not have (`job-where`), an order by nothing or by a list or a picture (`job-sort`), an act the app does not have or one that acts on another kind (`job-act`, `job-act-kind`), a role it does not know (`job-role`), a name used twice (`job-name-taken`) and more than twelve (`jobs-too-many`). A page reads `purpose` loosely, as it reads `connections`; a hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (81 bytes, 30 gzipped; handed a compiled app 135 and 66, and a visitor's form handed one 68 and 29), under raises measured beside the friction log's (`scripts/budget-raises/the-jobs-with-the-friction-log-*.json`); its shape is the checker's (`PurposeSpec`) and the document's JSON schema says it to an editor. Four edits change it — `set-purpose`, `add-job`, `edit-job`, `remove-job` — each judged as the checker judges and said ("A new job, "What's cheap?": answered by the vendors where cost < 500."), and the edits that rename or remove carry jobs along: a renamed kind, field, relation or act is renamed in a job's kind, `where`, order and act; a removed kind takes its jobs, a removed field or relation takes the jobs whose `where` reads it and the order by it, and a removed act leaves the job asking without it. `@graview/core/jobs` is a new entry: `jobsOf(app)` (each order with its direction, the kind's plural beside it), `jobsFor(jobs, principal)` (a seat's roles) and `answerJob(store, job, principal)`, which answers `{ count, ids }` read through `store.seenBy(principal)`, so a record kept from the seat is never counted or named and an archived one is set aside. `graview describe` opens with "Who it is for" (the sentence and each job's ask, only a seat's own under `--as`), and `aiContextOf` takes the app's `purpose` and tells a model `for` and the jobs whose roles include the viewer's and whose kind they may see (`state.purpose`); the inline AI hands it the app's (`InlineAsk.purpose`). The studio carries an app's purpose through, a renamed kind's jobs following it; `toDocument` writes it, leaving out a next act the document could not carry. The todo example says it is for a household and asks "What's late?" and "What still needs a slot this week?". The graview-new-app skill says it. Core's tests hold that a purpose is read from a document and from `defineApp` alike and given back, that each code is said at its path, that a job is answered in its order held to the seat's sight, and that every edit carries jobs along or lets go of what a removal takes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration — additive: `purpose` on a document and `GraviewApp`, and `set-purpose`, `add-job`, `edit-job` and `remove-job` among the edits. Check finding codes — additive: `job-kind`, `job-where`, `job-sort`, `job-act`, `job-act-kind`, `job-role`, `job-name-taken`, `jobs-too-many`. The JavaScript API — additive: `AppPurpose`, `JobDeclaration` and `JobSort` on `@graview/core`; the `@graview/core/jobs` entry (`jobsOf`, `jobsFor`, `answerJob`, `jobIsFor`, `MOST_JOBS`); an optional `purpose` on `AiContextOptions` and `InlineAsk`, and `purpose` on `AiContextState`; a "Who it is for" section at the head of `describeApp`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; `describe_app` says an app's purpose through `describeApp` as it is, a seat's model is told it once Cloud hands `aiContextOf` the app's `purpose`, and `propose_change` takes the four new edits through `editDocument`.

## 0.1.54

### Patch Changes

- b22d435: A field typed together stays open while a tour, a dialog or a popover holds the keyboard. Graview Cloud's typed-together harness found the live editor closed, and its caret lost, when a first-look tour opened over it: the tour took the keyboard, and the editor read any keyboard leaving its text area for anywhere but its own buttons as the person leaving the field. Now a keyboard that goes to a layer over the page — Graview's own overlays (`data-graview-overlay`, the first-look tour among them), a `dialog`, `role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"`, a popover — leaves the editor open, the caret where it was in the text area; given back, the person types on. Where the layer closes and leaves the keyboard on nothing, the text area takes it back, its selection as it was; where the keyboard goes from the layer to somewhere else on the page, the editor closes as before. `primitives`' test opens a tour over the editor and gives the keyboard back, closes a dialog without giving it back, and moves on from a dialog to the page, and the editor stays open with its caret, takes the keyboard back with its caret, and closes. `pnpm verify typed-together` puts a tour's hint over Ada's editor in a served preview, its button focused, and takes it away: the editor stayed open, the text area has the keyboard back with her caret where it was, and what she types next lands there (`aTourThatTakesTheKeyboardLeavesTheEditorOpenAndGivesItsCaretBack`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas, the JavaScript API, the theme and the embed — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing in its code; its typed-together harness's first-look case holds once it builds on this.
- cdef481: A host says where its history stops, and the timeline says it quietly rather than as a refusal (FR-213). Graview Cloud's Free plan keeps the full history for 30 days and refuses `archived` behind that, so a Free app compacted on ops 60 days old, scrubbed behind the horizon, captioned "The archive could not be read" over an alert. Now `HistoryHost.kept` (`{ since, sentence, action? }`, the type `KeptHistory` on `@graview/core/history`) says the time a host keeps its history since: playback never asks `archived` for a range made wholly before it — judged from the checkpoint's time, the log's and every op read so far — and the timeline says `sentence`, with `action` as a link, in a quiet line (`timeline-kept`, no alert) when it stands at a moment made at or before it. An answer of `archived` may say `keptFrom` (`ArchivedOps.keptFrom`): it holds no op before that seq, so the scrubber steps the past behind it by the epochs the archive handed, and a moment between them is before what is kept — `askArchived` answers `{ seq, behindKept: true }`, the caption says "Nothing this far back is kept" and the picture holds — never a refusal, and never asked again. `keptOf(store, host)` on `@graview/core/past` says what playback has learned so far: the stops behind the horizon, where it reaches change by change from, and the last moment made at or before `since`; `Playback.kept` carries it to the timeline, and `Playback.archive` gains `behindKept`. Core's test steps an archive that kept two epochs behind its `keptFrom` and asks nothing of one kept since after every archived change; primitives' draws the line with its link and no alert, and steps from the kept epoch. `pnpm verify playback` scrubs a host keeping a plan's window to the epoch its archive begins at, drawn as it stood (`theScrubbersFirstStopIsTheEpochTheArchiveBeginsAtDrawnAsItStood`), says the plan's line with its link and no alert (`aHostsKeptWindowIsAQuietLineWithItsLinkNotAnAlert`), and asks nothing of a host whose history is kept since after every archived change (`aRangeWhollyBeforeTheKeptTimeIsNeverAsked`). `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-213.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-213. Additive on `@graview/core/history` (`HistoryHost.kept`, `ArchivedOps.keptFrom`, `KeptHistory`), `@graview/core/past` (`keptOf`, `KeptPast`, `askArchived`'s `behindKept` answer) and `@graview/react` (`Playback.kept`, `Playback.archive.behindKept`). A hosted page carries 33 bytes more up front, within its budget (the scene face before it draws measures 1,023,948 B). What Graview Cloud does: hands the page `history.kept` with the plan's window start, its sentence and a link to its plans, and answers `archived` for a range behind the window with `keptFrom` (what `archivedSeenBy` answers from an archive that begins at an epoch, FR-214) rather than refusing it.

## 0.1.53

### Patch Changes

- 642145e: A visitor's form loads light (FR-201). Graview Cloud's form page — `@graview/embed/form` over the app its host compiled, read with `appFrom` from `@graview/core/compiled`, and the store the visitor is served (`openRemote`) — loaded 668 KB up front with React, more than the whole app shell: the derived form imported its controls, and the form its theme, from `@graview/primitives/pages`, the barrel every routed page draws from, and a bundler places in what a page loads first every module its imports reach that anything in the bundle uses. Through the barrel came the bar, the lists, the arranging, the framework's views of a kind and, through them, the document's blocks, view checker and expression parser. Now they import from `@graview/primitives/form`, a new entry with what a form draws with and nothing else (`Files`, `LinkedPicker`, `RepeatPicker`, `WhenInput` and `themeBaseCss`). Cloud's form page loads 308,835 bytes of Graview first (113,728 gzipped), from 468,438 (178,330): about 509 KB with React, from 668. What is left of the document is what builds and runs a compiled app (`document/compiled`, the evaluator and the template): an act the page applies before the server answers is judged as there. The form alone loads 235,371 bytes (86,563 gzipped), from 390,538. "A visitor's form alone" is lowered to that with its raises (226,800 / 83,600 base, from 380,000 / 146,000), and "a visitor's form handed a compiled app" is a budget of its own at 311,000 / 114,800, held by the embed's budget test under 400 KB and under 60% of the pages face. `pnpm verify address` opens the form over a compiled app read with `appFrom`, and it refuses an answer that does not fit and lands one that does (`aVisitorsFormOverACompiledAppRefusesAndAnswersAsBefore`). The bundle measure and `verify-address` now build with React's automatic runtime, as every package's tsconfig says: esbuild does not read it for a `.tsx` its source plugin resolves, and the form drew with a `React` that was not there. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-201.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-201. Additive on `@graview/primitives` (the entry `./form`). What Graview Cloud does: nothing in its code; its form page's 680 KB lint budget (`workers/cloud/lint/no-studio.test.ts`) can come down to about 520 KB once it builds on this.
- 42009a4: The scene keeps up with a hand on a large app, and the ground's grid moves with the picture. A product owner on an app of a thousand records and four thousand links found the scene slow and laggy, and the grid snapping back into place after every pan. A drag sets no React state now: whether a hand or the wheel is moving the picture is a ref the renders read, and what a person sees of the hand — the grabbing cursor, no text selected under it, the signs at the edge put away (`data-graview-hand` on the ground) — is written on the ground outside React, as the transform the hand paints is. The first move of a drag no longer draws the whole scene, and not one commit happens while the hand moves. The lattice the city stands on is an element of its own now, the last on the ground (`.graview-lattice`, where the ground's `::after` was), and a hand dragging the picture says how far it has taken it on that element alone (`--graview-hand-x`/`-y`, added to the lattice's anchor), in the same frame as the transform and taken off in the commit that moves the anchor by as much: the grid moves with the cards on it while they are dragged and does not jump when they are let go, and the frame costs one element's style, not the two thousand a custom property on the ground would reach. The fine square measure under the cards on the ground is part of the picture too: it was the ground's `::before`, fixed to the window while the cards slid over it, and is now an element of its own, the first on the ground (`.graview-measure`), anchored where the world is moved to — a layout says its pan at every altitude now (`Layout.pan`, tweened by `interpolate` with the cards) — and moved by a hand in the same frame as the cards; its fade stays the window's. With the ground's grids no longer pseudo-elements, axe can judge the contrast of what stands in the scene, which it had been reporting as undecidable, and it found the "+N more" card's words 4.0:1 on the receded shelf: they are in ink now, not its muted tone. Letting go of a drag, or the wheel coming to rest, draws the scene once: `useAnimatedLayout` draws a cut in the render that asks for it, rather than putting it into state from an effect a render later, a hand's pan is cut to without the second look a cut otherwise gets (`TransitionOptions.settle`), since the offset was painted where the cut puts every card before the hand let go, and turning motion back on with the layout it has just cut to no longer flies a half-second tween from that layout to itself — thirty-odd renders of the same picture after every drag. The pan no longer lays the city out again (the layout is keyed on the stop less its pan, by what it holds), and the cards in the scene and what floats over the picture — the panel beside the map, the menu at the pointer, the quick ask, the past — read the context free of the pan (`PanFree`, `usePanFreeContext`): kept while only the pan moves, so a board of 250 cards is not drawn again to be shown somewhere else, and a write of the whole view from in there keeps the pan the view has now. A presence's `sharing` is kept while it says the same, so a pan is not a new context for everyone; a band's card and the "+N more" card are drawn again when their picture changes, not when a pan moves them. What a selection reaches is worked out once per selection however many cards ask, its rules judged once per change of the graph — choosing a district had every card walk every edge and judge every rule for itself — and a record's connections read the rules judged once too. A plot drawn again for a zoom names its blocks from the names it said before; the scene stops asking where the ground is on every move of a drag, and says where the hand let go when it does; and the embed's `data-graview-drawn` no longer comes off for a pan, which is not another place (the place watched is the stop less its pan; `onDrawn` is not told the same place again). Measured on a made-up field survey of the same shape at a quarter of the CPU's speed: letting go of a drag 306 ms of main thread before, about 120 after; the wheel 366 → 125; letting go over a board of 250 records 307 → 110, nothing on the board drawn again; a drag's forty moves 413 → 365 with the grid now moving in them; choosing a district lost the 0.66 s every card spent working out what the selection reached. The pages and a hosted page carry 0.3 KB more up front (the presence's kept `sharing`, the drawn signal's place, the layout's `pan`), under raises of the hosted page's budgets, the pages face alone's and a visitor's form alone's (`scripts/budget-raises/the-scene-keeps-up-with-a-hand-*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 999 KB. React's tests hold that a cut is drawn in the render that asks for it and once, that nothing flies to where it already is, that what is inside `PanFree` is not drawn again for a pan and keeps the camera when it writes the whole view, and that what a selection reaches is worked out once for twenty cards. `pnpm verify scene-at-scale` serves the survey with `graview preview` and holds, against a reference measured in the same page: no React commit while a drag moves, from altitude and on the ground; every grid under the picture — the lattice and the measure from altitude, the measure on the ground — moving exactly as far as what stands on it mid-drag, and none of it moving when it is let go; letting go drawing nothing inside a card, under its budget; the wheel likewise; and going into a district of 250 records under its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `PanFree` and `usePanFreeContext` on `@graview/react`, and `TransitionOptions.settle`; inside `PanFree`, and inside the scene's cards, `useGraview().view` carries no `pan` (the pan is the scene's). The JavaScript API also gains `Layout.pan` and `InterpolatedLayout.pan` on `@graview/layout`. The theme — the ground's lattice is `.graview-ground > .graview-lattice`, no longer `.graview-ground::after`, and its fine measure `.graview-ground > .graview-measure`, no longer `.graview-ground::before`; a host's own rule over either moves with it. The embed — `data-graview-drawn` and `onDrawn` no longer go round again for a pan alone. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a host that styles the ground's grids itself moves its rules to `.graview-lattice` and `.graview-measure`.

## 0.1.52

### Patch Changes

- 821be82: A view hidden from the place menu leaves the bar, even while it is the place open (Roadmap f-hidden-view-leaves-the-bar). Hiding the place you were on — a kind's list on Pages, a picture on the scene — listed it under the drop-down's Hidden and still offered it on the bar's row and in the drop-down beside it, because a place kept off the lists was listed "while it is where you are". Now a hidden place is offered nowhere on the bar, wherever you are, at once and without a reload; you stay on it, at its address, and Show puts it back. Saying where you are is not offering it: on a hidden place the bar is its one control at every width, naming the place you are on (`app-place-current`), and the place is no entry of the row or the drop-down. Every bar reads one predicate, `offeredPlaces` on `@graview/core` (the places a list offers: none `hidden`), as do the scene's tabs (`Places`). Embed's tests hold that a kind's list, a declared lens and a view the host keeps, on Pages and on the scene, leave the bar when hidden while open, the bar still naming where you are, and come back on Show; `pnpm verify drawn` holds it in a real browser at a phone and a desk, mounted as Graview Cloud mounts an app (`aViewHiddenFromThePlaceMenuLeavesTheBarAndComesBackOnShow`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `offeredPlaces` on `@graview/core`; `barPlaces` marks a hidden kind's list `hidden` as before and now lists a hidden picture too, marked `hidden` (it left it out), and `scenePlacesOf` marks the hidden picture in view `hidden`, so a host that draws its own nav from either filters with `offeredPlaces`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; it draws no nav of its own, and `open_app`'s places already say `hidden`.
- 1a65f86: A list is exported as it is shown (Roadmap f-view-export-csv). A kind's list and a lens that lists records — a calendar, a timeline, a coverage, a board, a status board — offer a quiet, short "↓ CSV" press named "Export CSV" — beside a kind's heading, after adding one, and at the end of a lens's arranging line, and the place menu offers it too: either writes the records the place keeps, in its order, with its filter, its words and its groups — a group drawn shut included, since shutting one is how it is drawn — a "Group" column first when it is grouped, then each record's name, then the fields its page reads, computed ones too, each said as the page says it (money in the app's currency, a day in its locale, a choice as declared), headed by the fields' labels. Never more than the seat sees: the rows are the records the seat is served, so a field its sight keeps is in no row, and a field in no row is no column. The file is named for the place and the day ("Questions — 2026-10-23.csv"), with what a file system refuses replaced; the browser downloads it, or a host that saves files itself passes `onExport(csv, filename)` to the provider, `PagesApp`'s context or `mount`. Whoever may change the declaration is offered "Turn off export" in the place menu, and "Turn on export" where it is off: `export: false` on a lens (`LensDeclaration.export`) or on a kind's list (`pages.lists.<kind>.export`) offers it to nobody, set by the edit `set-lens-export` (`{ title, on?, export }`, on a kind's list by its kind or plural; refused on a lens that lists no records), read by a lens as it is drawn and by a list from the declaration, said by `diffDocuments` and `graview describe`, and written by `toDocument` only when it is off. `graview check` warns of an `export` that is not true or false (`lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`) and of one on a lens that lists no records (`lens-export-lists-no-records`). The list page draws its rows from `shownList` on a new `@graview/core/shown` — the horizon unless the past is asked for, a rank worked out over what the seat sees, the declared groups in order with the closed ones shut — and the export writes the same rows, so the two cannot disagree. The writer is on a new `@graview/core/csv`, fetched with the first press: `toCsv` (RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, a byte-order mark so a spreadsheet reads accents), every cell that a spreadsheet would run as a formula — starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return — written with an apostrophe in front (`csvSafe`), `listCsvRows` and `listCsv`, and `csvFileName`. The studio's lens takes `export`, and its change is said as `set-lens-export`. The graview-lists skill says how. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (the provider's `onExport`), under raises of the hosted page's budgets and the pages face alone's (`scripts/budget-raises/a-list-is-exported-as-it-is-shown-*.json`; the pages face's is mostly `shownList`, which the list now draws with), a visitor's form alone carries 6 bytes more and needs none, and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 996 KB. Core's tests hold that the writer quotes, guards and is read back by `parseCsv`, that the rows are the page's order and groups, a shut group's rows among them, that a field the seat's sight keeps is no column, and that the switch is edited, checked, carried, written and said; embed's, that Export CSV is on a list's line and in the place menu, hands the host the rows the page draws, and is offered to nobody where it is off, with the owner's switch.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged; `KeptViews.lens` may be handed `set-lens-export`. The wire — additive: `/graview/views` takes `{ lens: { op: "set-lens-export", title, on?, export } }`. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: `export` on a lens and on `pages.lists.<kind>`, the edit `set-lens-export`, and the findings `lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `lens-export-lists-no-records` and `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `@graview/core/shown` (`shownList`) and `@graview/core/csv` (`toCsv`, `csvCell`, `csvSafe`, `CSV_BOM`, `listCsvRows`, `listCsv`, `csvFileName`); `LensDeclaration.export`, `KindList.export`, and the `set-lens-export` member of `LensTidyEdit`; `OnExport` and the provider's `onExport`; `PageContext.onExport`; `FrameOptions.onExport`; `ArrangeBarProps.end`, `ArrangingOptions.exportable`, and `ExportPress`, `useShownListExport`, `shownListSaid`, `exportShownList`, `PlaceExportContext` and their keys on `@graview/primitives`; `saveShownList`, `switchExport`, `ShownListSaid` and `ListWords` on `@graview/core/csv`, where a list hands in how a record is said (`labelOf`, `readableFields`, `withComputed`) so the module fetched with the press imports none of them. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a host that saves files (or a guest frame that may not download) passes `onExport` to `mount`, and its views route passes `set-lens-export` to the declaration as it does `set-lens-hidden`.

## 0.1.51

### Patch Changes

- 920641e: A field typed together is edited live: the fourth phase of collaborative text, the editor. Under a host that carries typing — an embed handed a remote store, whose `remote.typing` the frame hands the provider as `typing` — prose the declaration types together (a Markdown field, a long text, a field marked so) opens with no draft and no Save: each keystroke is the field's at once, sent through `remote.typing(node, field)` as a splice read from the text area before and after it, the end it keeps never left of the caret, so a letter typed into a run of the same letter lands where it was typed. A run ends on a blur, Enter, an arrow or a click in the text, and by itself after 400 ms or a word. "Done" closes it, and so does Escape, which puts nothing back; Ctrl or ⌘ Z takes back this person's own last run of typing into the field and nobody else's, though they typed after it, and Ctrl or ⌘ Shift Z puts it back. Somebody else's typing lands in the text area as it arrives, the person's selection carried over it, a caret staying before text inserted where it stands; while an input method composes a word nothing is sent and what others type waits, and the two meet when the word is chosen. Markdown keeps its Preview, read from the live text, and the scene's record and the routed page are one binding. Where the host carries no typing, or the field is written whole, the draft and its Save are as they were. A presence may say where somebody's caret stands in such a field (`caret: { node, field, anchor, head, rev }`, offsets against their log at `rev`); the editor says the person's while it holds the keyboard, at most every 120 ms, and draws everybody else's as a thin line in their hue with their name on it, laid on a copy of the text that wraps as the text area does, carried over the typing the page's log took since they said it. A server keeps a caret only when it is a record, a field and three counts, and tells it to no seat whose sight keeps that record or that field from it (`presenceSeenBy(who, sees, seesField)`). `@graview/ship`'s handler loads `@graview/core/typing` itself, so `graview serve` and `graview preview` answer `type-text` as the main entry does. The editor is fetched when such a field is opened — with `@graview/core/typing`, which says whether the field is typed together — and keeps its own diff and carry rather than take `@graview/core`'s, so a page carries 0.4 KB more up front (the provider's `typing`, `TypingContext`, the presence's caret), under raises of the hosted page's budgets, the pages face alone's and a visitor's form alone's (`scripts/budget-raises/a-field-typed-together-is-edited-live-*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 994 KB. Primitives' tests hold that a keystroke is sent as a splice and nothing is saved, that a composed word is held and lands with what others typed meanwhile, that a remote splice keeps the caret on its word, that Escape does not put anything back, that ⌘Z takes back only the person's run, that others' carets are drawn and carried, and that prose written whole keeps its Save; ship's, that a caret is told only to a seat that sees its field. `pnpm verify typed-together` types into one long brief from two seats at once with real keystrokes, in three engines: both pages and the store converge, each draws the other's caret where they stand, and a caret stays on its word.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. The wire — additive within protocol 1: a presence may carry `caret`, which a server of this build keeps only when well formed and withholds from a seat that may not see its record or field, and which a server before it passes on as any other field a client said; `WIRE_PROTOCOL` does not move. Snapshot and log formats, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `PresenceCaret` and `Presence.caret` on `@graview/core`; the provider's `typing`, `TypingContext`, `FieldTyping` and `LiveTyping` on `@graview/react`, and `PresenceState.sayCaret`; `EmbedRemote.typing`, called without its remote; `presenceSeenBy`'s optional `seesField`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a host that mounts the embed over its own remote passes that remote's `typing` (an `openRemote` store's own is passed already) and a field typed together is edited live; a host that tells who is here itself and judges sight passes `seesField` to `presenceSeenBy`.
- 0956b46: Typing is said in words: the fifth phase of collaborative text. A person writing a paragraph sends a run of typing every word, and each was a row of its own; now a run is said as what it wrote, where and how much — "Ada wrote in Kitchen's notes (+42 −3)" — and one person's consecutive runs into one field are one thing. A keystroke's own sentence (`type-text`'s intent) says "Wrote in Kitchen's notes (+5 −1)". The timeline folds them into one tick (`ticksOf`: `batches`, every gesture's batch, and `typed`, the field and the counts; "Restore to here" stops before or after a run, not inside it), Activity into one row with one undo that takes the run back whole (`foldDigests`' row says `typed`, and `typedRowWords` on `@graview/core/tidying` says it), and `historyInWords(store, { me?, limit?, context? })` on `@graview/core/history` is a history newest first in sentences — what an agent's history answer says — each run of typing with the spans it put in and took out (`typedSpans`), never the field's text. `typedRun`, `typingWords`, `typingSentence` and `typedCounts` are there too. A notification says a field typed into as what was put in and taken out (`MatchedRecord.fields[].typed`: `{ inserted, removed, spans }`, with no `before` or `after`) rather than the whole text twice, and is told once, when the typist stops: `holdTypingRuns(hook, { quietMs })` on `@graview/core/subscriptions` holds a match that is a run of typing and joins the same person's next run into the same field for the same subscription into it — one match, every batch (`SubscriptionMatch.batches`), the counts added — until `TYPING_QUIET_MS` (5 s) pass with no more, or another match for that subscription comes, which is told after it. `watchSubscriptions` (`typingQuietMs`) and `@graview/ship`'s handler (`typingQuietMs` beside `onSubscriptionMatch`) hold through it, and tell what they hold when they stop or close. The app as it stood partway through a run folds the text typed so far (`pastOf`), and an agent reads a field typed into whole, as before. `PagesApp` takes `typing` (and `context.typing`), handed to the provider it puts under the routes, so a host that draws the pages alone, with no embed around them, edits a field typed together live; a short string stays a whole value with a Save, which the graview-pages skill says why. The scene face fetches 1.5 KB more before it draws (Activity's fold and its words), and its limit before it draws moves to 995 KB; the pages face alone carries 0.2 KB more, under a raise (`scripts/budget-raises/typing-is-said-in-words-pages-face.json`). Core's tests hold that a run is said with its counts, that one person's runs are one tick and another's breaks it, that the history in words says spans and never the text, that Activity folds a run, that the past partway through a run holds the text so far, that a notification says spans, and that a run is told once its typist stops and before what comes after it; tools', that an agent reads the field whole and its history in sentences; pages', that the pages alone edit live when handed `typing`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged; a `type-text` op's intent reads "Wrote in …" where it read "Type in …", and an intent is display only. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `PlaybackTick.batches` and `.typed`; `historyInWords`, `ChangeSaid`, `typedRun`, `typedSpans`, `typingWords`, `typingSentence`, `typedCounts` and `TypedRun` on `@graview/core/history`; the `typed` row of `foldDigests`, `TypedRow` and `typedRowWords` on `@graview/core/tidying`; `holdTypingRuns`, `HeldTyping`, `TYPING_QUIET_MS` and `WatchSubscriptionsOptions.typingQuietMs` on `@graview/core/subscriptions`; `SubscriptionMatch.batches` and `MatchedRecord.fields[].typed`, where a spliced field used to carry its whole text as `before` and `after` (since collaborative text's phase 2, never released); `typingQuietMs` on the store handler's options; `PagesAppProps.typing` and `PageContext.typing`; `UndoTurn` takes several batches. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its `history` tool answers with `historyInWords` (or says a tick's `typed` with `typingWords`), and its notifications are told a run of typing once — a host that matches commits itself puts `holdTypingRuns` between `matchSubscriptions` and its delivery, and reads `fields[].typed` for a field typed into.

## 0.1.50

### Patch Changes

- a2ae894: A text field takes a splice: the first phase of collaborative text, the primitive and the transform beneath it, with no act, wire or editor yet. A sixth primitive, `{ op: "splice-text", id, field, at, removed, inserted }`, changes part of one text field — at `at`, in UTF-16 code units, the field holds `removed`, and `inserted` takes its place — and `PRIMITIVE_OPS` names it, at op format 3 (`FORMATS.op`; `upgradeOp(op, 2)` reads a format-2 op as written, and a build before this one refuses a store stamped 3, or a splice, with `NewerFormatError`, as #374 made it do). It keeps the text it removed, so its inverse is the same splice turned round (`invert`) and an undo puts the text back; `Graph.applyPrimitives` folds it all or nothing, judges the field's new text as a patch's would be judged, and refuses one whose text is not there — a field that holds no string, `id` or `kind`, an offset past the end, or other text at `at` — as a corrupt op. It writes its record (`writesOf`) and moves its field's revision (`FieldRevisions`), so a whole write made against the text before it is stale, and claims no base of its own. Everything that read a patch reads a splice: the diff of what changed, a seat's view of the log (a splice whose field is kept from the seat is no part of it, one whose text names a record the seat may not see is withheld, and where the seat is served the field otherwise it is said as a patch of the field whole), who wrote each field, a subscription's matched fields (said whole, before and after), an answer's records, a sync engine's first-run values, a migration's fold of a stored graph (`applyToSnapshot`), `graview apply`'s summary, a record's untrusted fields for a model, and a kind's count of changed records. The transform is `@graview/core`'s: `applySplice(text, splice)`, `transformSplices(a, b)` (both outputs, so either order gives one text; where two splices touch, both insertions stay, the one further left first and, at one offset, the one earlier in the log first), `composeSplices(a, b)` (a run of keystrokes as one splice, none when they do not touch), `transformCaret(caret, splice, bias)` and `diffSplice(before, after)` (never halving a surrogate pair). Core's tests hold them by property over seeded cases: 4,000 pairs of concurrent splices converge, 4,000 splices and their inverses put the text back, 300 logs of 25 splices made against older texts fold to one text every time, 4,000 runs compose, 4,000 carets keep their word and 4,000 diffs make one text the other; a format-2 fixture reads as written. The fold and its readers are on every page with the store: 1.9 KB more up front on a hosted page and on one handed a compiled app, and about 1.8 KB on the pages face alone and on a visitor's form alone, under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-text-field-takes-a-splice-*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 981 KB; Cloud's worker imports measure 2,005,731 B, under the 2,250,000 their budget is raised to once for the road to 1.0.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — additive: `splice-text` is a new primitive at op format 3; every op this build or an earlier one wrote folds as before, and nothing writes a splice yet (the `type-text` act is the next phase). Stored formats — op format 3: a store this build writes is stamped `op: 3`, which a build before it refuses to open with `NewerFormatError` rather than misread; rolling back means refolding from the log on the older build. The wire — a client now says `opFormat: 3`, which every server since 0.1.43 reads as "sends ops bare", and no server sends a splice yet. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build before any of its stores is written by it, since a store stamped 3 does not open on an older worker, and keeps a default in any `switch` on a primitive's `op`.
- 5edb2e7: The Suggested tray offers Not now beside Accept and Decline. It hides a suggestion from the person who pressed it until tomorrow, and keeps that as a `Dismissal` in the host's store of the person's own state (`dismissals`: `{ list, dismiss, onChange? }`, a `DismissalStore`, on `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PagesApp`). Without one, the tray keeps it in this browser: localStorage where the page may use it, else the page's memory. The log says nothing of it, so the suggestion still waits for everyone else and the app's own actor is held to nothing; Decline still answers for everyone. What the button does is said to a screen reader, and when a row settles the keyboard moves to the next row, or to Activity, never to `<body>`. Given what the app's own actor did while the person was away (`whileAway`: `{ tidied, waiting? }`, which `whileAway()` from `@graview/tools/tidy` returns), the tray says "One suggestion waiting · tidied 12 things while you were away", and it still draws when nothing waits but something was done. A seat gets the same answer as a tool: `withNotNow(runtime, dismissals)` from `@graview/tools` lists `dismiss_suggestion` (with `until`, an ISO time, if the person names one) and leaves what the person dismissed out of `list_suggestions`. The chat and the agent seat wrap their runtimes with it. It wraps the runtime instead of living in it, so the names Graview Cloud's worker imports stay at 1_999_652 bytes of their 2 MB. Activity folds everything one run of the app's own actor applied (`Lineage.run`) into one row, wherever a person's change fell between its batches, and says a run made overnight that ended today as "Tidied 12 things overnight". Where an actor gave no run, consecutive batches fold as before. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front for it (105 bytes, 51 gzipped), and the scene face 480 more before it draws.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `dismissals` and `whileAway` are optional props, a host that passes neither gets the tray in this browser, and `dismiss_suggestion` is listed only by a runtime wrapped with `withNotNow`. The digest row a run makes is new: Activity rows for an actor that gives no `run` fold as before, and `ActivityRow`'s digest gains an optional `run`. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format and the tool schemas a bare `createToolRuntime` lists are unchanged.
- 46dc821: The formats fail closed for 1.0: what a reader does not know it refuses, or reads as the app, and two writers on one log fail loudly. A fold switched on the five primitives with no default, so a build that met a primitive a newer one wrote skipped it and folded a different graph from the same log without a word. Now an unknown primitive is refused with `NewerFormatError` (`unknown: 'primitive "splice-text"'`) wherever ops are folded — opening a log, receiving or rebasing onto ops, `Graph.applyPrimitives` (all or nothing), a seat's view of the log, a migration's fold, `upgradeOp` — and `PRIMITIVE_OPS` names the five. A field value that is an object with a `$type` is a typed value, reserved as `$archived` is: applying one whose type the build does not know (`VALUE_TYPES`, none yet) is refused and the record left as it was, while the log, every adapter, an export and the wire pass it through as written. An author's kind is open the other way: `AUTHOR_KINDS` adds `visitor`, which `formVisitor` now mints for one answer to a form (it said `human`), and a kind a build does not know reads as `system` for display and policy (`authorKindOf`) and is never refused; Activity, history and a link's provenance say a visitor as a person, and a seat header naming an unknown kind is a person's seat. An op's `intent` is display only: ship read a migration's version out of its sentence, and now reads it from its batch. Refusal codes are open: `REFUSAL_REASONS` adds `unauthenticated` (401) and `quota` (403), which a host's `limit` or `admit` says as `{ refuse, reason }` (`HostRefusalReason`; `limit`, 413, when it names none), and a client treats a code it does not know as a final refusal, as `openRemote` already did. `PersistenceAdapter.appendOps(scope, ops, { head })` takes the head the writer last knew, and the memory, SQL, SQLite, file and browser adapters refuse an append that finds the log elsewhere with `LogHeadMovedError`, appending nothing; `openStore` passes it on every write and reports the refusal through `onPersistError`, writing nothing after it, so a second tab or process no longer writes a second op 1 that made the next open refuse the store. A `SyncState` is stamped `format: SYNC_STATE_FORMAT` and a newer one is refused. `scripts/lib/surfaces.mjs` now names sync, subscriptions and their file, a host's views and their file, presence and the refusal codes, and `docs/stability.md` says the JavaScript APIs are held to semver from 1.0. Core's tests refuse a log with `splice-text` instead of diverging, refuse a `$type` it does not know and keep one it only passes along, read a visitor's op as a person's and an unknown kind as the system's, and stamp and refuse a sync state; the adapter contract refuses the second of two writers at the same head on every adapter; ship's tests fail the second of two stores on one scope over memory, a file, localStorage and IndexedDB, and take a refusal code a client does not know as final on the socket and over HTTP. The checks are on every page with the store: about 1.1 KB more up front on a hosted page and one handed a compiled app, 0.8 KB on the pages face alone and 0.4 KB on a visitor's form alone, under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/formats-fail-closed-*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 979 KB; Cloud's worker imports stay under 2 MB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — additive: no primitive and no field is added; a primitive or a typed value a build does not know is refused rather than skipped, and every log this build or an earlier one wrote reads and folds as before. Author kinds — additive: `visitor` is new, and a visitor form's ops say it where they said `human`; a reader before this one reads such an op's author as a kind it does not know. Stored formats — additive: `SyncState.format` is new and optional, and a state without it is format 1; `appendOps` takes an optional third argument, and an adapter that ignores it works as before. The wire — additive: `unauthenticated` and `quota` are new codes, said only when a host's `limit` names them; the rule that no code is added within a major is replaced by "a client treats an unknown code as final". What Graview Cloud does: says `{ refuse, reason: "unauthenticated" | "quota" }` from its `limit` where it said `limit` with a sentence, and keeps a default in any `switch` on a refusal's reason or an author's kind.

## 0.1.49

### Patch Changes

- 69b9046: Enter on a kind or one of its pictures in Find takes the keyboard out of the box in the same press. Since 0.1.47 the keyboard went to the kind's place on the first frame the place stood, but until then Enter left it on nothing, and the Shell's rule that the keyboard always lands somewhere gave it back to the box: a key pressed in that frame — the place's acts key, typed straight after Enter — still went into the words. Enter now puts the keyboard on the place at once where it already stands, and otherwise on a holder beside the box, said to a screen reader as "Opening Songs…", until the place stands; from there it goes on to the place and its arranging line as before. A place that never stands lets the keyboard go after 240 frames, and the root gives it back to the box. Primitives' tests find the keyboard off the box in the press of Enter, still off it after the root's rule has looked, on the place once it stands, and on a place that already stood without a frame between.
  
  Compatibility: a behavior change in the scene's Find only: between Enter on a kind or a picture and its place standing, the keyboard is on a visually hidden status beside the box (`data-testid="find-landing"`) rather than on nothing. The API, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing.

## 0.1.48

### Patch Changes

- 29f5e0d: A declared row names its record. Threat Watch's threat row was set to `{severity} · {status} · …` alone, and its list drew lines of facts with no threat named in any of them — a list of links nobody could tell apart — while describe_place said each record's title above them: the words and the picture disagreed. A row is a link to its record and needs a name, for a reader and for a person: one whose blocks show no `title`, no `headline` and no words holding the record's whole name is now drawn with the record's name first, as a title is; one that names the record is drawn as declared (`blocksName`, on `@graview/core/blocks`). describe_place says the name that leads the row, then what its blocks say — a row led by a headline is said with the headline as its title, once, where it was said as the record's title and then as a heading. `graview-view-specs` says a row is always named, and how to choose how. Primitives' tests find a row of facts alone drawn with its record's name first and said with it, and a row led by a title, by a headline or by words that hold the name drawn as declared with the name once. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front for this and the abbreviations of `a-creating-form-asks-its-name-first` (0.4 KB on the page handed a compiled app), the scene face 1.2 KB more before it draws, and the pages face alone 1.6 KB more (0.8 KB gzipped), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-declared-row-names-its-record-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the declaration, stored formats, the wire, check findings and derived tools are unchanged. A row spec that names its record nowhere now draws the record's name before its blocks; describe_place's item for a row led by a headline has the headline as its `title` and leaves it out of `parts`. New on `@graview/core/blocks`: `blocksName`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its pages and describe_place agree once it takes this version.
- 7c9b1ae: A headline that opens on a count in words starts with a capital, and an acronym is never lower-cased inside a sentence. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, headed its home "{count(all('indicator'))|words} indicators…", which read "seventy-two indicators…" unless its author knew to add `| capital`. A headline that opens on an interpolation formatted `words`, `plural` or `relative` — words the framework wrote, not a person — now starts with a capital when its first word is all lower case: "Seventy-two indicators…". Only a headline: text the author wrote first stays as written ("only three left"), and so does a record's own name ("iPhone", "eBay"), which is a person's spelling, not a sentence's. Separately, a kind whose noun is "CVE" and plural "CVEs" was written "cVEs" in a change's summary ("How cVEs work out…"), in a place's description and in the chat's sentences: each lower-cased a plural's first letter as a word inside a sentence reads. `lowerFirst`, on `@graview/core/words` with `capitalize`, never lower-cases a word whose second letter is upper case, and every place that lower-cased a noun, a plural, a field's label or a refusal's first word reads through it or the same rule. `core`'s tests say the review's headline "Two findings, worst first" without `| capital`, leave text the author wrote first and a record's name as spelled, and say "How CVEs work out" in a change's summary.
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `capitalize` and `lowerFirst` on `@graview/core/words`. A behavior change in words: a headline opening on `words`, `plural` or `relative` starts with a capital, and a noun or plural written as an acronym keeps its capitals inside a sentence. The declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
