## 0.1.30

### Patch Changes

- 6cbecc0: A card says its kind's glance and no more, the bar names a place once, and a drawn home stays Home beside `pages.first` (the same review app). A finding card read "Critical · Security · High" — the third its confidence, though a severity can be high too — and still did after `confidence` was taken out of the kind's `glance`: the glance chose the order and the rest of the kind followed it. A glance a kind declares is now said exactly, and a choice another field shown beside it shares says its field ("Confidence: high", "Likelihood: low"), on a card, a row, a describe line and a table alike. A default card no longer draws a long or Markdown text as a chip cut after a couple of dozen characters: it says the text's start on a line of its own under the chips. With a home declared and `pages.first: "This week"`, the app opened on This week, and a press on Home from the scene drew the pages at their first entry and went to This week again, so the home the author drew was nowhere: `pages.first` now picks where a new visit lands, once, and after that `/` is the home on both faces. `graview check` says so when both are set (`pages-first-beside-home`, a note). And a kind whose plural is "root causes" beside a blocks place titled "Root causes" put two "Root causes" on the bar: the place keeps the name and stands, as `pages.first` and `pages.primary` already read the word, and the kind's list folds into More as "All root causes", still at `/root-causes`; `graview check` warns of it (`pages-place-named-as-a-list`). The graview-pages and graview-lists skills teach each. With the template fixes beside them, a hosted page carries 1.2 KB more up front (and 0.6 KB more up front handed a compiled app).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. New check finding codes `pages-first-beside-home` (a note) and `pages-place-named-as-a-list` (a warning). `readableFields` with `glance: true` says only a kind's declared `display.glance` when it has one, and a choice's `alone` may carry its field's label. `barPlaces` labels a kind's list "All <plural>" and folds it when a place has its name (`allLabel` from `@graview/core`). `@graview/react/provider` exports `hasLanded(views)` and `markLanded(views)`, which the pages and the embed read `pages.first` once by. A default card's prose is `p[data-graview-excerpt="<field>"]`.
- 6862f3a: A calendar of years spans its records, from the first to the last. A career calendar opened on 1970-04-10 with a horizon of thirty years drew 1970–1999, and a discography's releases of 1962–1969 and 2000–2023 were nowhere. Its years now reach back to the first record and on to the last as well as across the horizon from the day it opens on — "The career · 1962–2023" — and a planning calendar whose records stop short still looks out as far as its horizon. A horizon says its ends outright with `from` and `to` (years), which win; a horizon written as a number is its years, called "Years"; and one that is not a span of years is a warning by its path (`lens-horizon`) and offers none. The graview-lens skill says the options, and `verify-declared` holds the span in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A calendar lens's `horizon` takes `from` and `to` beside `years` and `title`, and a number; a document's lens `options.horizon` is held to that shape by its reader. `spanOf` takes `fit` (the records' first and last years). A new warning code, `lens-horizon`.
- ec39547: A change made away from this keyboard is said aloud whether or not anybody is shown here. It was said from the bar's "who is here", which is drawn only once somebody is, so a change arriving from a served store with nobody shown present — presence off, or the other person gone — redrew the page in silence. The bar keeps one polite status of its own, empty, always there; an op that is not this reader's fetches what says it (with the bar's panes) and it is said once, with who made it: "Ben Okafor: Changed the stage of Billing across apps to Shipped". With somebody shown here it is still said once, never twice.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 0.3 KB more up front for it, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/a-change-from-elsewhere-is-heard-a-hosted-page.json` and `…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. The region is `.graview-bar-said`, `role="status"`; the `[data-testid="here-changed"]` region inside who is here is gone.
- 0d45ba0: A coverage grid arranges its rows before it draws, as the board, the timeline and the calendar do. A kind whose stop is a coverage grid — the discography's songs, a rescue team's callouts — had no Filter on the scene at all, so narrowing them by a number or a choice could be done only on the pages. The grid draws the arranging line over its rows at full size, filters the rows (its columns stay whole), and offers no sort or grouping, which a grid has no place for.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 6862f3a: A coverage with nothing to cross says so instead of listing its rows. "Songs by genre" — songs against genres through `inGenre` — with no genre yet drew forty songs as a plain list with no columns, which read as the answer to a question it could not yet ask. With no columns it says "No genres yet: link songs to genres.", and with columns and not one link over its relation "No song is linked to a genre yet: link songs to genres."; it draws no row, and a glance at it says "nothing linked". `describePlace` says the same words. A coverage over a path still draws its grid of empty rows, since the records between can be ones a seat may not see, and a filter that keeps only rows with nothing against them still draws its grid. The graview-lens skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds it in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. `coverageUnlinkedWords` is new on `@graview/core`.
- 825838f: A calendar's month grid tells the days of the next or last month by a muted ground rather than fading them to 45%: their numbers read at 1.9:1 against the page, under the 4.5 a person needs, and axe said so on graview.dev's tour.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- f901dfb: A district's panel beside the map is one landmark for its kind. The panel is a region named for the kind ("Plots"), and the list of its members inside it was a second section with the same name: one place said twice to a screen reader, and axe's landmark-unique on every page that opened a district beside the map (the garden's chapter seven on graview.dev among them). The list is a named group inside the region now, not another region; it is still entered and announced as "Plots". `a-district-panel-is-one-landmark-for-its-kind` holds it.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 6862f3a: A field called `label` is an ordinary field where a kind is named by another. A release kept its record label in a field called `label` ("Parlophone") and was named by its `name`, and every release in a coverage grid read "Parlophone": the grid took a record's `label` value before the kind's own naming. The grid, in the lens and in `describePlace`, now names a row as everything else does, by the kind's label. A kind with no label template is named by `name`, then `title`, before a field called `label` among the fields that could name it (required ones first, as before), and a `label` field the kind is not named by is said as "Label" and shown among the record's facts like any other field; a kind named by `{label}` keeps it as its name.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A document kind with no `label` template whose fields include both `label` and `name` or `title` among the same required (or unrequired) word fields is now named by `name` or `title` where it was named by `label`; a kind that names itself with a template, or whose only such field is `label`, is named as before. `defaultLabelField` says the new order.
- e46f304: A figure may be said large. In a home whose headline wears a display face, a stat tile's number was drawn at a figure's modest size with no way to say it was the point of the page. `{"figure": "count(all('finding'))", "label": "findings", "emphasis": "large"}` draws it at the size of the home's own heading, in the display face (smaller on a card or a row). The check refuses any other word at its path (`view-emphasis`) and refuses `emphasis` on `{"figure": true}`, the kind's picture. `FIGURE_EMPHASES` is exported beside `FIGURE_FORMATS`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a figure block takes a new key, `emphasis`, whose one value is `"large"`, and a new code, `view-emphasis`, refuses another. A document that does not say it is unchanged. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- dc5a737: A new app can point a person at its own controls once. A host that wanted to introduce the switch between the scene and the pages, the problems count, Activity and the Ask box could only do it by their selectors, which are not a surface it may hold the framework to, so its tour broke the first time the bar was drawn differently. `firstLook` on `mount` and `<Embed>`, the whole-page `Shell` and `PagesApp` runs a short sequence of hints the framework anchors to its own controls: one at a time, beside the control each names and never over it, a non-modal dialog the keyboard moves into and is said as it opens, with Next, Done on the last, Skip and Escape; a press anywhere else ends it, and the keyboard goes back where it was. A hint whose control is not drawn — no Ask box, nothing yet in Activity, the switch gone from a phone's bar while Find is open — is skipped, and it works on either face of an embed, kept across a change of face. It is shown once per viewer: the host says whether this viewer has seen it and is told when it is over; with no host seam, the browser remembers it in `localStorage` for the app. A host adds hints of its own after the framework's by naming one of the framework's controls. The hints are fetched only when a first look is shown: a hosted page's first load is about 1.1 KB more (0.4 KB gzipped), inside its budget, and every face 7.6 KB more minified.
  
  Compatibility: additive. New: `firstLook?: FirstLook` on `FrameOptions` (so `mount`, `<Embed>` and `@graview/embed/pages`' `mount`), `ShellProps` and `PagesAppProps`, where `FirstLook = boolean | { seen?: boolean | (() => boolean); onDone?: (how: "finished" | "dismissed") => void; steps?: readonly { anchor: FirstLookAnchor; title: string; words: string }[] }` and `FirstLookAnchor` is one of `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS`: `"faces"`, `"problems"`, `"activity"`, `"ask"`, `"find"`, `"profile"`, `"home"`. `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS`, `FirstLookDoor`, `firstLookKey` and the types `FirstLook`, `FirstLookAnchor`, `FirstLookEnd`, `FirstLookOptions`, `FirstLookStep` and `FirstLookDoorProps` from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`; `FIRST_LOOK_ANCHORS` and those types from `@graview/embed`. Off unless given: an app that passes nothing draws and loads what it did. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool names and schemas are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: pass `firstLook: { seen: () => <the viewer has seen it>, onDone: () => <record that they have> }` to `mount` for a new app, and name an anchor rather than a selector in any hint of its own; `true` remembers it in the browser instead.
- 6862f3a: A coverage orders its rows and its columns, and a status board its cards, as the declaration says. "Releases by era" listed its releases in the order they were written. A coverage lens takes `sort` for its rows and `columnSort` for its columns, and a status board `sort` for the cards in each column, written as a kind's list writes its order: a field (`"released"`), or `{ "by": "released", "direction": "desc" }`, with `label` the record's name; numbers as numbers, words and dates in the page's order, and a record with none last. Within a coverage's row groups the order holds before its `rowRef`. The lens, `describePlace` and the checker read one comparison (`sortForGrid`), and an order by a field the kind does not have is a warning by its path (`lens-sort`, "release has no field "year" to order by …") and draws as written. A document's reader holds the shape. The graview-lens skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds the order in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A document's lens `options.sort` and `options.columnSort` are held to a field or `{ by, direction: "asc" | "desc" }` by its reader, where any value was passed over before; the coverage and status board lenses take `sort` (and the coverage `columnSort`) as options. `sortForGrid`, `gridSortOf`, `gridSortProblem` and `GridSort` are new on `@graview/core`. A new warning code, `lens-sort`.
- 5b8ac1f: A lens can be kept off the lists (Roadmap f-hidden-lenses). A kind could be left off the home, but a lens could not be left off anything, so an app whose custom home links to lenses as targets ended with a long page list of them. A lens marked `hidden: true` is still a place at its address — a link, a view's `navigate` and `/places/<as>` reach it, and `placesOf` lists it with `hidden: true` — but the bar's places, the scene's tabs (unless it is the one shown), the page list and the home's gallery do not offer it, and it never becomes what its kind draws when an address names no picture. In a document `set-lens-hidden` sets it — `{ "op": "set-lens-hidden", "title": "Building now", "hidden": true }`, with `on` where two lenses share a title — and says so ("The lens "Building now" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it.", and "… is listed on the bar and the page list again."), as `diffDocuments` does; a lens drawn anew in a hidden one's place (`add-lens` with `replace`) stays hidden, and a lens `pages.primary` stands on the bar is refused, in a sentence, until it is taken out of `primary`. `graview check` says a `hidden` that is not true or false (`lens-hidden-not-a-boolean`, a warning), a hidden lens with no title to be a place (`lens-hidden-untitled`, a note), and `pages.primary` naming a hidden lens (`pages-primary-hidden`, a warning). In TypeScript a lens says `hidden: true` beside its `title`, and a registration `views.register(kind, cell, View, { title, hidden: true })`. The studio reads a hidden lens as hidden and keeps one off the lists, or lists it again, with its own act, said to the document as `set-lens-hidden`. The graview-pages and graview-studio skills teach it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the wire are unchanged. The document format takes `lenses[].hidden` (a boolean, optional within `graview-document@1`); a build that predates it draws the lens listed. New edit op `set-lens-hidden` (`title`, `on?`, `hidden`); `EDIT_OPS` lists it. New check finding codes `lens-hidden-not-a-boolean` (a warning), `lens-hidden-untitled` (a note) and `pages-primary-hidden` (a warning). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The lens "…" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it." `LensDeclaration.hidden`, `DrawnLens.hidden`, `Place.hidden`, `ViewMeta.hidden`, `ViewRegistration.hidden` and `AppPlace.hidden` (now a lens's too) are `boolean | undefined`. The studio's meta-schema gives `lens` a `hidden` field and the act `set-lens-hidden` (`id`, `hidden`).
- 6862f3a: A list shows more past a page, and groups by a linked record. A list block held at most 100 records and a `limit` past 100 was refused, so 158 songs had to be split by their names into two lists; and a list grouped only by a choice field, so songs by genre, where a genre is a record, could not be said. A list now holds up to its `limit` (1,000 at most, the most it holds unsaid), draws a page of 100 and a "Show 58 more" button that draws the next page — under their headings, where it is grouped — and a list cut past what it holds still says "See all" or "and N more". `group` takes a relation as a kind's list writes a field: `"inGenre"`, or `"out('inGenre')"` and `"in('writtenBy')"` to say which way, a heading per linked record in the order of their names, a record linked to two under both, and one linked to none last, under "No genre". A group by what is neither a choice nor a relation of the kind listed is refused by its path, as before. The graview-lists skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds both in a browser.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 4.0 KB more up front for these and the other fixes from the Beatles' discography (a coverage that says it has nothing to cross and orders its rows and columns, a career calendar's span, a field called label kept a field, a place known by its title), and 3.3 KB more where it is handed a compiled app, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/lenses-and-views-a-hosted-page.json` and `…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`; the embed's pages face, every face, the embed with the studio handed in and two of the guest host's budgets are raised in their own files beside them.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A list block's `limit` is a whole number from 1 to 1,000 where it was 1 to 100, and its `group` may name a relation; a document that compiled compiles. `MAX_LIST_LIMIT` is 1,000, and `LIST_PAGE` (100) is new. A resolved list (`ResolvedList`) keeps its first page in `members` and the records after it in `rest`, with `everyGroup` over them all where it is grouped; `more` counts the rest; `ListGroupOf` names a group's shape, and `groupRelationOf` reads a group by a relation.
- 057931a: A long name edited in place keeps to its box. The field took its value's length in characters as its width, so renaming a sixty-character callout on a phone made a field a thousand pixels wide and the page scrolled sideways under the person typing. The field grows with its value only as far as its box, and may shrink.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- e46f304: A headline inside a group is a section heading, not the page's title. The home's first headline is the page's heading, and a headline at the top of a column inside a row group was the first of its own blocks, so it was said at that level too: a home with two such columns drew two more titles at page-title size, each with the app's description repeated under it. A headline in a group or a condition is said at the level of the view's sections now (on the routed face's home, an `h3` under the page's `h2`), and the description is said once, under the real title. It is not a level more per group: a row of columns is layout, and a heading two groups in would skip levels in the page's outline.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. A resolved headline inside a group or a condition reports the view's section level (`ResolvedBlock.level`) where it reported the page heading's; the declaration is unchanged.
- 489ff27: A number is narrowed by typing it. The Filter list offered a number only as the round steps its records happened to make, a heading opened and walked down, and nothing at all where every record held the same number. Typed into the list's own box, "35" offers "at most 35 min" and "at least 35 min" first, one key away, and a number with no steps is still found that way.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- e46f304: A place may leave off the forms that begin its kind. Every place's page ended with the create form for the kind it stands on, which on a page that is read, not written (a summary, a report) is a form nobody came for. A lens says `"create": false` and its page offers none; the kind's list and the bar still do. A per-place key rather than a form folded behind a button, because the author knows which places are read: a button would still put an act on every summary, and a place that is written keeps its form open as before. The document's shape types it (`lenses[].create`, a boolean, in `document.schema.json`), `graview check` warns at `lenses.<i>.create` when it is not one (`lens-create`), `add-lens` takes it ("its page offers no form to add one"), and `DrawnLens.create` carries it to whoever draws the place.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a lens takes a new key, `create`, a boolean; a document that does not say it is unchanged, and one that says something other than true or false is refused by the document's shape, or warned of (`lens-create`) in a TypeScript declaration. The `add-lens` edit takes `create`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 489ff27: A kind raised as a relation is a way into it. From todo's "The lists", pressing the Tasks district raises the tasks as a relation of the lists, which is what a press there means, and then nothing on the screen went into them, where their list is sorted and filtered: only a double-click nobody is told about. The raised chip on the picture's trail is now two controls: the kind's name, "Go into the tasks", and its ×, "Stop showing tasks".
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `data-testid="raised"` is the × alone, and the way in is `data-testid="raised-into"`.
- 489ff27: A record's card reads down when it is narrower than its two columns. Seedbed's plot card on a phone held its facts and its relationships side by side, 200 and 220 pixels in a card 293 wide, and its relationships ran past the card and the screen, cut off. The two stand side by side, in the same proportion, where both fit, and one under the other where they do not.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 312521c: A record chosen on the scene, in a box too narrow for anything beside it, says "Acts…". Its acts opened only by a right-click or the acts key, and a finger on a phone has neither: choosing a list lit it, and "Add a task" was on no screen. On the ground (the map's panel already says it from above), the one record chosen gets a button in the picture's column of controls, under Up and the key, that opens the same menu; it floats, and takes no room from the picture.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 37745ff: A relation can go to a kind in another app, and the linked record is read there as whoever is looking (FR-171, the full relation). 0.1.29 named another app; this names a record in it. A relation's `to` may be `"@<app address>/<kind>"` — `"forHouse": { "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one" }` — and `linkedRecordField("@nick/house/house")` says the same in a declaration, which `toDocument` writes back as the relation. Linking picks a record there and never touches the other app: the record that links keeps which one under the relation's name, as `"house:h1"` (or a list), so its derived edit sets it, undoes it, logs it and keeps it from a seat a sight keeps it from like any value. `graview check` says in sentences what such a relation cannot be — `linked-mixed`, `linked-apps`, `linked-target`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`, `act-linked`, `rule-linked` — with the checker, so a page that compiles without it carries none of their words. The host reads the far side beside `resolveApp`: `linkedRecords` on `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face answers `resolve(ref, viewer)` with the record's label, describe line, card and the fields that viewer may see, `{ denied: true }` or `null`, `find(search, viewer)` with the records that viewer may link, and `open(ref, viewer)`, optional, the seam a press on a linked record opens it through. The record's page and the scene's record panel draw it with its own card from its home app, and a document card's `{ "field": "forHouse" }` names it; a viewer the host refuses sees "linked record (no access)" and nothing of it. "Change" beside it, a form and the scene's ask pick another from what the host lists. The rule language walks it by name, `forHouse.listPrice`, in a computed field, a block or a rule, as the viewer: a far record it cannot read reads as nothing and is said — a computed value says why it has none, a rule that would not hold without it cannot be judged — and never thrown; the page reads again as each answer arrives (`store.reread()`). When the far value changes, the host calls `recordLinkedChange(store, { ref, fields, app })` and the reading app's history keeps an op authored by the other app on each record that links it — "Buy it outright: the linked record changed in House — list price" — naming what changed and none of its values. `describe_app` lists each relation under "Linked from other apps" with how to link one, the derived edit's tool says its argument is a record of that app as `"<kind>:<id>"`, and a seat finds one with `find_linked`, listed only for an app with such a relation. `@graview/core/linked` holds `linkedRecordsOver`, the host over stores held in memory, read through each far store's `seenBy(viewer)`, which the tests read two compiled documents through and a host holding both apps can use as it is; `graview-linked-records` is the skill that teaches it. A hosted page's first load is 5 107 bytes larger (550 088 over the vendors document, 544 981 on main), 4 648 handed a compiled app, both under their budgets; what draws a linked record is a chunk fetched with the first one drawn, and the embed's budgets are raised by files of their own: the pages face 4 585 / 1 704 bytes minified / gzipped, every face 20 162 / 7 423, the studio handed in 13 618 / 4 936.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains a relation target `"@<app address>/<kind>"` in `kinds.<kind>.edges.<name>.to` (`document.schema.json`'s `edges.to` items match a kind name or that target), its value kept on the linking record as `"<kind>:<id>"` or a list of them, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses such a target by path. The check gains `linked-mixed`, `linked-target`, `linked-apps`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`, `act-linked` and `rule-linked`. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `linkedRecords: { resolve(ref: { address, kind, id }, viewer): Promise<{ address, kind, id, label, describe?, card?, fields, href?, app? } | { denied: true } | null>; find({ address, kinds, query, limit }, viewer): Promise<LinkedRecord[] | { denied: true }>; open?(ref, viewer): void }`. Ops and primitives are unchanged: a far change is an ordinary op appended with author `{ kind: "system", id: "app:<address>", name }`, `via: "app:<address>"`, the linking records in `reads` and no primitives. `ReadableField` gains `linked`, a form gains the control `linked`, a document card's resolved field block gains `linked`, and the derived `edit-<kind>` of an app with such a relation describes its argument with `"x-graview-linked"`; an app with none has the same tools as before. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing such a relation, pass `linkedRecords` to have a linked record drawn and walked, and call `recordLinkedChange` when a far value changes.
- 84ad32f: A record's change starts from what the record holds. "Change the feature" opened with "—" in every field, on a record page and beside the act in the scene, so a person changing one fact could not see the others and a screen reader heard "Stage, —, pop-up button". Every field of the derived edit now opens on the record's value — a text, Markdown as written, a choice, money as it is typed ("12,500" beside its "$"), a date and how it repeats, a link, an app, and a record in another app — and only what was changed is sent, so saving one field leaves the rest as they were and the change says only that field. In the scene, how a date repeats is asked with the picker the pages ask with, never as a box to type in, and a choice the record holds is marked among the others (`aria-current`).
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `@graview/core/forms` exports `editStart(mutation, graph, args)`, the record's values for the fields its derived edit asks, and `changedArgs(args, start)`, what a form that started there sends. Derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged.
- e46f304: A row group lays any blocks side by side. `{"group": [...], "direction": "row"}` put four figures in a row, but four toned lines of text, or two column groups of meters, each took the whole width and stacked, since in a wrapping row a paragraph's natural width is the whole line. Every block in a row group takes a share of the line now (a figure a narrower one, a badge only its own width, a headline or a divider the whole line) and the row wraps on a phone before anything is squeezed. `pnpm verify declared` measures it at a desk and a phone.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- e46f304: A list drawn as rows gives each record the list's width and its whole name. `{ "list": "all('finding')", "as": "row" }` over a kind with no row of its own drew each record as the scene's chip, about 240 pixels wide with its name cut at about thirty characters, leaving a page 1440 wide mostly empty. The chip's cap is right in a band slot of the scene and wrong in a list; each record is a line of the list now, its name whole and wrapping where it must. A kind that declares its own row still draws with it.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- b6c81b2: A card in the scene says there is more at the edge there is more past, and its "+N more" opens the rest (Nick, on a module's card in Graview Cloud). A card that scrolled painted a strip of its own ground stuck to its bottom, so its last row of chips sat under the fade however far it was scrolled, and its scroll shadows, painted in the scroller's ground, stood as a dark band across the middle of the card, over its description and its summary, once scrolled. The edge now says it: the card's scroller runs to the card's own bottom edge, and a shadow is cast inward from the bottom while rows stand below and from the top once scrolled from it, measured, drawn under the rows and gone at the end it is scrolled to (`data-graview-more-below`, `data-graview-more-above`). "+2 more" under a card's related records was a chip with no press behind it; it is a button (`aria-expanded`) that opens every chip of the group in place and closes again as "Show fewer". `a-cards-edge-says-there-is-more-without-covering-a-row` and `more-on-a-card-opens-every-chip-and-closes-again` hold them, and `verify-drawn-once` asks both of a hospital's systems review mounted as Cloud mounts a document (`aCardsEdgeSaysThereIsMoreWithoutCoveringARow`, `moreOnACardIsAButtonThatOpensEveryChipAndClosesAgain`).
  
  A chosen record's ties on the ground keep their words until they are asked for. Every tie said its relation at its far end all the time ("the findings in it: 7 findings"), so a few ties were a row of floating labels over the cards, one over a district's name and another over a finding's title. The words now show while the tie, or a card at either end of it, is under the pointer or holds the keyboard; shown, they stand beside the far end on no card's title where there is room, and words two ties would both say are said once. They were drawn always so that a chosen record's links said what they were; they still do, a pointer or a key away. `a-ties-words-wait-to-be-asked-for` and `aTiesWordsWaitUntilItIsReachedAndStandOnNoTitle` hold it.
  
  A record's own view in the focus frame leaves the record under it whole (Nick, on The Beatles app at `#focus=album:beatles-for-sale`). A view of one record draws the record's own card under it, and that card held itself to its box inside a frame already held to the box, so the grid stacking the two gave the card what the view left: "Made by" and the chip under it were cut to a sliver. A panel inside the frame's body is drawn whole now, and the body is the one thing that scrolls. `a-records-own-view-leaves-the-record-under-it-whole` and `aRecordsOwnViewLeavesTheRecordUnderItWhole` hold it.
  
  Together they add 4.5 KB to what the scene loads before it draws (913_594 B; its ceiling is 893 KB), 0.9 KB to the pages face alone and 4.4 KB to the embed with the studio handed in (scripts/budget-raises/a-scene-card-says-more-at-its-edge-pages-face.json, a-scene-card-says-more-at-its-edge-studio.json).
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- c4a953d: What a screen reader is told, stop by stop. `verify-screen-reader` walks the Roadmap and the todo example through the accessibility tree Chromium and WebKit expose — the bar, the Pages and Scene switch, a list, a record page, a columns lens, a blocks lens, an act's form, the Ask box, and a change from someone else in another tab — and writes what a reader would hear to `docs/screen-reader-walk.md`. What it found is fixed where it was drawn:
  
  - A place followed from the bar puts the keyboard on the new page's heading. The bar's link stays on the page, so the keyboard stayed on it and the page under it changed in silence; only a person typing (the Find box, the seat, a field) keeps the keyboard where it is.
  - A change somebody else makes is said aloud as it lands, politely, with who made it: "Ben Okafor: Change Billing across apps: stage → …". Their ops only, never this reader's own or an agent's acting for them, and never one withheld from this seat; the keyboard stays where it was.
  - A card in a list or on a board says its name once, first, as the way to it. The link stretched over the card comes before it in the page, and the card's own title in the same words is drawn and not said again (`NamedByItsLink`).
  - A value changed in place says which fact it is: "Stage: Shipped", never "Shipped, button". A heading's words are said as they are (`EditableValue`'s new `said`).
  - A district's way in is named in words from altitude ("See the features in here"), never by its arrow alone.
  - A record's act takes the keyboard into the form it opens, and Escape puts the form away and gives the keyboard back to the act.
  - The arrow beside a picture a record is seen in is named by the picture ("Roadmap, in the scene"), not "↗"; the arrows after "In the scene", "as a list", "Connections" and "Sort and filter" are drawn and not said; a flagged row says "Breaks a rule:" before its name rather than "⚠"; a required field's star is drawn and not said ("Name", required — not "Name star"); and the separators between related places and in the arrange bar are no longer read as words of their own.
  
  Bundles: every face is about 2.6 kB larger minified (0.9 kB gzipped) and the embed with the studio handed in about 1.7 kB (0.5 kB), raised in `scripts/budget-raises/a-screen-reader-is-told-each-stop-*.json`; a hosted page's first load and the embed without the studio are unchanged.
  
  Landed beside graview.dev's scene fixes and a kind's list in its declared order, the three together carry 0.3 KB more up front on a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. `EditableValue` takes an optional `said`, and its button's accessible name is now the fact and its value; a test that finds it by its value alone finds it by `data-graview-field` instead.
- f7c1a9e: A record named in Activity goes to that record when pressed, and nothing else there looks pressable. The records under each change and each call were chips carrying a pick target that only the scene's own pointer answered, so on Pages, and from the keyboard on either face, they looked pressable and did nothing. A record still in the graph is now a button that goes to its page on Pages, or focuses and chooses it in the scene, and closes Activity as it goes. A record the change removed is named from the removal, where it used to show its id ("i2"), and is a plain label, as is an argument that is a word rather than a record. A design that replaces the routed face's shell under the bar (todo's) no longer shows two "Activity" buttons once there is a change: the derived bar drew Activity in its tools and also handed its own slot to the design's face controls, which drew it there too. It is now drawn once, in the slot.
  
  The every-face and studio budgets are raised by 1_169 / 470 bytes in their files.
  
  A change's preview no longer says a text field turned to Markdown "is now said as in undefined — 12 reads 12". `diffDocuments` said the number-format sentence for any change of format, and `formatWords` took `markdown` for a number's unit; the preview now says only that the field is written in Markdown, and adding a Markdown text field says "(text, written in Markdown)".
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. Activity's records carry `data-testid="activity-record"` and `data-record`, not `data-graview-pick`.
- cf85240: An app can name one standard emoji as its page icon and, where it has no logo, its mark. A brand's icon was an SVG or a path, so an app somebody made in a chat had a blank tab and an empty corner of the bar until somebody drew it a picture. `brand.favicon` now also takes `{ emoji: "🏡" }`, in a TypeScript `Brand` and in a document alike: exactly one emoji as the reader sees it (Intl.Segmenter), and one of the standard emoji every emoji set draws (`RGI_Emoji`), so a skin tone, a joined emoji and a flag — 🏡, 👩🏽‍🌾, 🇺🇸 — are each one, and two emoji, letters or nothing are refused in words: `"🏡🏡" cannot be the app's icon, because it is 2 emoji, and a mark is one` (`brand-emoji`, at `brand.favicon.emoji`), from `compileDocument`, `graview check` on a declared app, and `editDocument`. `set-brand` takes `favicon: { emoji }` and says "The app's page icon and mark become 🏡." (or "The app's page icon becomes 🏡." when the app has a logo), and the preview of a changed document says the same sentence. Where the brand has no logo, `AppMark` draws the emoji as text on the app bar of both faces and in an embed, and the routed face's static pages put it in the masthead; with a logo, the logo is the mark and the emoji is only the icon. `faviconHref` makes the icon an SVG with the emoji as text, so a self-hosted app's tab shows it in the reader's own emoji font; a host that wants it alike on every system renders it from a bundled emoji set into icon PNGs and serves those instead, as Graview Cloud does. New from `@graview/core`: `emojiProblem`, `emojiMark`, `emojiOf`, `emojiSvg` and the `FaviconSpec` type. The graview-brand skill says it in two lines. The every-face and studio-handed-in budgets are raised by 2_385 / 795 and 2_409 / 920 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-every-face.json, scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-studio.json); a hosted page's first load stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: the document format's `brand.favicon` gains a second form, `{ "emoji": "<one standard emoji>" }`, within `graview-document@1`; a document with a string `favicon` compiles to the same app. The TypeScript `Brand.favicon` is now `FaviconSpec` (`string | { emoji: string }`), so code reading it as a string narrows first. `set-brand`'s `favicon` takes the same object. Check finding codes gain `brand-emoji`. Ops, stored formats, the wire and tool names are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: render `brand.favicon.emoji` from its bundled emoji set into the icon PNGs it serves, and serve those rather than the framework's text SVG.
- e46f304: Badges that follow one another flow side by side. A card with three badges drew each on a line of its own, and so did a record's page, because a view is a column. A run of badges is one line now, wrapping when it is full, as chips do; a badge alone stands where it is.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. A run of two or more badges is drawn inside `.graview-spec-badges`.
- 6931bf7: From the map, the control that goes down says the picture it lands on. A kind with two titled pictures drew the last one registered, which the bar marked as where you were, while the control said "Close the map, down to" the first; it names the last, as the registry and the bar do.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 489ff27: Find costs the keyboard about what it costs the pointer. The gauntlet's longest title took 89 presses from the keyboard, every talk before it a row, and narrowing a list by a number took 62 against the pointer's 6, most of them Tabs. PageDown and PageUp walk the strip a page of records at a time; an answer of more than twelve records says "another word of the name narrows them"; Enter on a kind goes into the kind whole, where it used to narrow it by its own name ("talks" kept ten talks of 1,960); and where Enter went the keys go, onto the place's arranging line, rather than being let go on nothing. Home and End stay the words' own: the box is a field being written in.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- 91716ae: The past and the app's own actor's suggestions leave the store for entries of their own, so a hosted page carries neither before somebody looks back or something waits. Each of 0.1.30's changes fit the hosted page's budget alone, and together they were 10.0 KB over it. `store.at(when, options)` and `store.seqAt(when)` are now `pastOf(store, when, options)` and `seqAt(store, when)` on `@graview/core/past`, beside `pastAt(store, when, host)` and `declarationAt`, which leave `@graview/core/history` for it; the provider fetches the entry the first time somebody asks to see the past, and the timeline's helpers stay on `/history`. `store.suggest`, `store.suggestions`, `store.acceptSuggestion`, `store.declineSuggestion` and `store.declined` are now `suggest(store, calls, options)`, `suggestions(store, principal?)`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?)`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?)` and `declined(store, calls)` on `@graview/core/tidying`; the bar fetches the Suggested tray, and with it what reads which suggestions still wait, once the log holds a suggestion. Asked of a seat's view (`seenBy`), each reads the whole store and answers as that seat, as the methods did. What they do is unchanged.
  
  That took 3.7 KB (1.2 KB gzipped) off what the hosted page loads first. What stays up front is what the page draws or judges with before anything is asked — the store's holding of the app's own actor to what people said, its refusal of a change to the past and the seam every face reads the moment from, a relation to another app compiled as its field and walked by the rule language, an emoji drawn as the app's mark, and the first look's door — and the hosted page's budget is raised by 7.4 KB (2.5 KB gzipped) up front to 544.9 KB, and the page handed a compiled app's by 5.3 KB (1.4 KB gzipped) to 501.3 KB (`scripts/budget-raises/the-0-1-30-wave-*.json`); every face's budget is raised by the 1.8 KB the changes measure together over their own raises.
  
  Compatibility: `Store.at` and `Store.seqAt` are removed for `pastOf(store, when: number | string | Date, options?: { declaration?: PastDeclaration }): Store` and `seqAt(store, when: string | Date): number` on the new `@graview/core/past`, which also takes `pastAt(store, when, host?: HistoryHost): Store` and `declarationAt(marks, seq)` from `@graview/core/history`; `Store.pastEvery` reads `StoreOptions.pastEvery`. `Store.suggest`, `Store.suggestions`, `Store.acceptSuggestion`, `Store.declineSuggestion` and `Store.declined` are removed for `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?: ApplyOptions): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?: ApplyOptions): ApplyResult` and `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` on `@graview/core/tidying`, which now exports `SuggestOptions` and `Suggestion` too (`Suggestion` stays a type of `@graview/core`; `SuggestOptions` leaves it). Ops, the wire and stored formats are unchanged.
- c70440c: A person can scrub back through an app's history and watch it being built (Roadmap f-playback). `pastOf(store, seq)` and `pastOf(store, time)`, on the new `@graview/core/past`, are the app as it stood then — its records, its links, its log and its problems — read, never changed: it offers no act, declared or derived, `permits` says "Nothing changes while you look back.", and every change asked of it is refused with a `PastError`. A seek folds from the nearest point it can, an epoch or a point kept in memory for that store every `pastEvery` ops (64) as an earlier seek passed it, so scrubbing never folds the whole log twice; a store reopened with its log but not the epoch its seed began (a page's browser adapter keeps none) works back from the graph it holds with each op's inverse, so the past begins at the seed rather than at nothing. `pastOf` asked of a seat's view of a store answers under that seat's sight, so a seat sees the past only as it could see it then. `seqAt(store, time)` says where a time falls, and `pastAt(store, when, host)` is the past under the declaration a host says the moment was made under (`declarationAt`). The past is an entry of its own, apart from the store, because a page needs none of it until somebody looks back: the provider fetches it then. `@graview/core/history`, fetched with the timeline, holds what the timeline reads around it: `ticksOf(store, { me, declarations })` is a tick per gesture marked by who made it (`you`, `person`, `agent`, `app`), with a bigger tick where the declaration changed and the sentence it said, from an epoch's `change` or a host's `DeclarationMark`; `declarationSentence` ("Added a Budget kind"), `makerOf`, `makerWords` and `restoreBatches`.
  
  On both faces, every row of Activity has "Show the app as it was here": the face draws the app as it stood just after that change, from the one store every surface reads, with a timeline over the scene and at the head of the routed face's page — "As of" the moment, what was done there and by whom, a tick per change, Play at 1× to 8×, and Back to now. While the past is shown nothing is offered to change it: no act, no undo on Activity, no ask field, no way back after an act, and a design's own control that asks `permits` is disabled. "Restore to here" takes back every change since as one undo, itself undone as one, offered only when this seat may make every one of those changes and the log lets them go together. An undo whose target also came after the earliest op being taken back no longer blocks it, since the two leave the graph as if neither was made.
  
  What the log does not hold is the host's: `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PageContext` take `history: { declarations, viewAt }`, the declaration changes it knows (each with the declaration the past is drawn under from its seq) and `viewAt(name, seq)`, a script view's code at a moment, `null` where the view did not exist then. `HostFiles.kept(href)` says whether the host still keeps a file's bytes, and one it has let go of is drawn as "removed later", as is a picture that does not load while somebody looks back. `verify playback` drives it on the todo example in three engines. Up front, the hosted page carries only the seam the faces read the moment from and a store's refusal of a change to the past; the fold, its kept points and the timeline are fetched when somebody first looks back (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`). The every-face, pages-face, studio and registering-a-worker-view budgets grow by 13_243 / 5_442, 4_630 / 1_928, 5_790 / 2_173 and 482 / 154 bytes in their raise files.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives fold as before (`OperationLog.fold` lands each op through the new `foldOp`, unchanged); `checkUndo` no longer counts as blocking a live undo whose target is itself after the earliest op being undone, so an undo that was refused with "Include batch …" over such a pair now runs, and nothing it was allowed before is refused. New API: the `@graview/core/past` entry — `pastOf(store, when: number | string | Date, options?: { declaration?: PastDeclaration }): Store`, `pastAt(store, when, host?: HistoryHost): Store`, `seqAt(store, when: string | Date): number` and `declarationAt(marks, seq)` — and `Store.past`, `Store.pastEvery`, `StoreOptions.pastEvery`, `PastError`, `PastPoint`, `PastDeclaration`, the `@graview/core/history` entry (`ticksOf`, `declarationSentence`, `makerOf`, `makerWords`, `restoreBatches`), `HostFiles.kept`, `history` on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` and `PageContext`, `usePlayback`, `useLiveStore`, `useOwnPlayback`, `PlaybackContext`, `PastBar` and `prefetchTimeline`.
- c3564eb: The app's own actor suggests what it is not sure of, is held to what people said, and its run of changes is one row of Activity. A background actor that links and prunes records on its own ("Graview, tidying") could apply a change with its lineage (FR-172) and nothing else: what it was unsure of had nowhere to wait, a person could not say "leave this one alone", an app could not say "never auto-link Vendors", and a pass of thirty links buried a person's own changes in Activity. An author may now say `automated: true` (a `system` or `agent` author: the app's own actor; `isAutomated`).
  
  A suggestion that waits is `suggest(store, calls, { author, lineage, via?, intent? })`, on `@graview/core/tidying`: the calls are judged as `applyAll` would judge them for that author, on a rehearsal, and none of it is applied — the log keeps one op that `suggests` them, with no primitives and its lineage, so it persists, travels and is withheld from a seat exactly as any op is. The same suggestion asked again answers the one that waits, and one that would change nothing is not suggested. `suggestions(store, principal?)` lists what waits, under the seat's sight (or the seat whose view `store` is); `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options)` applies the calls as the person, carrying the suggestion's lineage with `lineage.suggestion` naming it; `declineSuggestion(store, id, options)` is an undo of the suggestion, which keeps its lineage as `undoneLineage`. `declined(store, calls)` answers the op that declined these calls, or any one of them — a declined suggestion, or an actor's confident change a person took back — and the store refuses the app's own actor the same change or suggestion again, in a sentence ("Graview, tidying may not make this again: a person declined it on 2026-10-14."). The log is where suggestions live, rather than a seam a host fills, because accepting and declining are then a `call` and an `undo` like any other, and a host persists nothing new. Both faces show what waits in a quiet Suggested tray beside Activity on the bar — what it would do, who suggested it, its basis and how sure, Accept and Decline — fetched, with what reads which suggestions wait, only once the log holds one, so a hosted page carries up front only what holds the actor to what people said (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`).
  
  Leave this one alone: every kind is given `leave-alone-<kind>` ("Leave this one alone") and `allow-tidying-<kind>` ("Allow tidying again"), ordinary undoable acts that set and clear `$leftAlone` on the record as archive sets `$archived`, offered on the record's page and the scene's record card beside archive, and said at the head of the record's page and in its card. `isLeftAlone(store, id)` reads it. The store refuses the app's own actor any change that touches a record left alone — a patch, a removal, a link at either end — and a suggestion about one, naming the record. A declaration says what is never tidied: `tidying: "never" | "no-links"` on `defineNode` (a document's kind) and `tidying: "never"` on a relation; the actor is refused a change to a kind never tidied, a link to or from a kind never linked, and a link of a relation never tidied, each in a sentence, and may still suggest them for a person to decide. The document edit `set-tidying` (`{ kind, relation?, tidying: "never" | "no-links" | "allowed" }`) says it in a sentence, `diffDocuments` says the same sentence, and `graview check` refuses a grant naming a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied (`grant-act`) and warns of a relation that says what its kind already says (`tidying-redundant`).
  
  A digest in Activity: consecutive batches by one automated author fold into one row, "Graview, tidying Tidied 6 things today", which opens to each change with its own lineage and its own undo; a person's change and a chat seat's never fold, and a suggestion and its declining are the tray's, not rows. The agent's tools gain `list_suggestions` (the actor's basis handed over as somebody else's words), `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion`. `@graview/core/tidying` makes, settles and reads it all for a host and a face: `suggest`, `suggestions`, `acceptSuggestion`, `declineSuggestion`, `declined`, `suggestionsIn`, `declinesIn`, `declinedIn`, `suggestionKey`, `tidyingOf`, `tidyingRefusal`, `foldDigests`, `digestWords`, `isSuggestionBatch` and `LEFT_ALONE`. The embed's budgets for the pages face alone, every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/the-apps-own-actor-*.json`). The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `suggests` (on an op with no primitives, which folds to nothing) and, on a lineage, `suggestion`; authors gain the optional `automated`; a record may carry `$leftAlone: true`, written by an ordinary `patch-node` — additive within op format 1, and a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version. The wire is unchanged: accepting is a `call` whose lineage names the suggestion, declining an `undo`. The document format gains the optional `kinds.<kind>.tidying` and `kinds.<kind>.edges.<relation>.tidying`, the edit `set-tidying`, and the check finding `tidying-redundant` (a warning); `grant-act` also names a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied. Derived tools: every kind gains `leave-alone-<kind>` and `allow-tidying-<kind>` (`{ id }`) unless it is never tidied, and the agent's surface gains `list_suggestions`, `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` (`{ suggestion }`). New APIs: `isLeftAlone`, `isAutomated` and the type `Suggestion` on `@graview/core`, and `@graview/core/tidying` with `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` and `SuggestOptions`. Stored formats are unchanged.
- 01fa7c5: From the keyboard, Find's arrows go record to record. A record lit unfolds its acts under it, and ↓ walked into them, five keys a record, so a talk twenty records down was past the keyboard's reach. ↓ and ↑ go to the next and the previous record; → goes into the acts under the record lit, and ← back to it.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- c9d1840: The pointer's menu over a record says what one of its kind is called. Under a rope's name it said "kit", the kind's key, where the declaration calls one a "piece of kit" and every act in the same menu said so; the head reads each kind's `noun`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- eb3bdb7: A right-click on the pages opens the scene's context menu. It was the scene's alone, so on a list, a record's page or a picture's page a right-click met the browser's menu and the acts were a scroll away. It is the same menu now — the same pane, the same acts `@graview/tools` derives, narrowed by the seat, with what is withheld said the way the scene says it — opened on what the gesture landed on: a list's row, a record's page, a card or a link to a record, a lens's mark, all about that record, with "Open", "Open in a new tab" and "Show in the scene" beside its acts; a value on a record's page, with "Change “Due”" first, which opens that value's own edit in place; a picture's ground, about its place, headed with its title and offering what its kind offers ("Add a task"); and the page's own ground, with the list's kind's acts and Find. Shift+F10 and the context-menu key open it on what has the keyboard, and a finger resting on the page opens it on a phone (on a list that chooses, resting on a row still chooses it). The keyboard goes into the menu and back where it was opened; Escape closes it.
  
  The browser keeps its own menu in a text field or editable text, over text somebody selected, and whenever Shift is held with the right-click — the way to the browser's "Open link in new tab", which the menu also offers itself. A design's own value takes part by carrying `data-graview-field` beside `data-graview-editable`, and its own row by being a link to the record or carrying `data-graview-record`.
  
  A host adds a section of its own to the menu on either face: `menuSections` on `GraviewProvider`, on `embed()` and on the pages' context, each a function told what the menu was opened on (`MenuTarget`: `face`, `on`, `about`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `element`) that answers a `MenuSection` — rows (`items`: `{ id, label, run, hint? }`) or what it draws itself (`render(close)`), before the acts (`at: "start"`) or after them — or nothing. A face adds its own with `MenuSections` from `@graview/react/drawing`. `graview-desk` teaches the seam, and `graview-lists` how a design's own rows and values take part. The menu and its pane stay off a page's first load: they are fetched when the page is first idle, or at the first right-click, and a hosted page's first load carries nothing more for them. The embed's pages face alone carries 3.7 KB more in all (the menu's pane, now fetched there too), every face 0.5 KB and the embed with the studio handed in 5.0 KB, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/the-pages-open-the-scenes-context-menu-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. New on the provider, `embed()` and `PageContext`: `menuSections` (the context menu's section seam). New on `PointerMenu`: `about`, `face`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `title` and `returnTo`, all optional. `Inspector` takes `openActs`. The `acts` popover is drawn on the pages too.
- 489ff27: The past has a way in from the line that arranges a list. A rescue team with one callout under way and 1,458 behind it drew a picture of one row with no arranging line, and on a phone the district that says "+1458 past" was folded away with the others, so the past could not be reached or narrowed. A lens's line (`useArranging`) and a kind's stop now draw wherever records stand behind the horizon, with "+1458 past" on the line itself (`ArrangeBar`'s new `past`); pressed, it widens the view to the past and the keys go on along the line.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `ArrangeBar` takes an optional `past`.
- 489ff27: The scene's foot keeps its controls clear of the ask field, and one sign at the edge never stands on another. On a phone the ask field runs the foot's whole width, and the zoom (−, its level, + and "From above") stood under it, covered; the zoom and the signs along the foot now stand above it. Along the foot a sign was spaced 36 pixels from the last, however long its name; where more districts lay past an edge than it had room for, the last were all held at its end, one on another, so "← Plots" stood on "← Gardeners"; and the foot's far end is the zoom's. Each sign stands past the one before by its own length, and one with no room left on its edge is not drawn: its district is still panned to and listed with the folded ones. And what a chosen record's link says where it lands steps back onto the ground at the screen's edge: on a phone seedbed's "what it grows, year by year" ran past it.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.

## 0.1.29

### Patch Changes

- 7966f5d: A change says what it was based on, and a link made less than sure is drawn as such until a person keeps it (FR-172). An author said who moved and nothing said why one change was made or how sure its maker was, so a background actor that links and prunes on its own ("Graview, tidying") could not show its reasons or be told apart from a person's work. An op may now carry `lineage: { basis, confidence?, model? }` — given as `ApplyOptions.lineage` to `apply`, `applyAll`, `applyPrimitives` and `undo`, or on an `AppendOp` — checked before anything is kept (a basis of 1–500 characters, a confidence from 0 to 1, a model of 1–120) and kept in the log, on the batch and on the wire. On the op rather than its author, because an author is the same for every change a seat makes and a basis is this change's own. It is said where the change is: "based on “both name 12 Harbor Street” · 60% sure · jev-1" on a record page's history line and in the scene's recent changes, and a record page says what the record was made on. A link whose making op was less than sure, by anyone but a person, is provisional: the scene draws it in short dashes and quieter, its basis on hover and in its acts when it is pressed or long-pressed, and a record page marks it "unsure", the mark saying the basis and offering "Keep this link". Keeping it is `keep-link` (`KEEP_LINK`), an act every store answers to and no list offers, which takes the link away and puts it back as the person's op; any person's op that takes the link away does too, and undoing the keep makes it provisional again. An undo of a change with a lineage keeps it as `undoneLineage`, so a host can read "do not make this again" — a merge (FR-173) as much as a link. `@graview/core/provenance` is the one answer every face and host reads — `linkProvenance(store, edge)`, `isProvisional`, `provisionalLinks`, `provenanceWords` — fetched with what draws a link, so the hosted page's first load stays under its budget. Nothing in the framework decides what to link.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `lineage` and `undoneLineage`, additive within op format 1: an op without them reads and folds as before, and a build that predates them keeps them on an op it receives. `Batch` gains an optional `lineage`. The wire's `call` and `undo`, on the socket and over `POST /graview/ops`, may carry `lineage`; a change whose lineage is not one is refused as `invalid`, and a server that predates it ignores the field. Every store answers to `keep-link` (`{ kind, from, to }`), which no list of acts — `allMutations`, `permittedMutations`, an agent's tools — offers; an app that declares its own `keep-link` keeps it. Stored formats, the document format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-172. What a host does: a background actor passes `lineage` with each change it makes, reads `linkProvenance` or `provisionalLinks` for what a person has not yet kept, and reads an undo's `undoneLineage` for what a person declined.
- a43f829: A count is said in the page's locale, and the map's district panel counts what its head counts (the ninth walk, W-198). `counted` — the sentence every face counts a kind with — wrote the number bare, so a beekeeping co-op's home said "7540 inspections" and its district panel "7347 inspections → 299 hives"; it now says the figure as FR-178 says every other ("7,540 inspections", "7.540" in de-DE). The panel's link counts are said the same way, and its relations are counted over the records its head counts — "280 hives → 14 apiaries" under "280 hives", where it said 299 by counting the dead-outs past the horizon.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and wire messages are unchanged. The words a person reads change: a count of a thousand or more is said with the locale's separators wherever `counted` is used (the home, a list's head, the map, a static page, the seat's lines), and the district panel's relations leave out records past the horizon unless the view shows the past.
- 55ec52b: A date can repeat: a weekly practice, a monthly bill or a yearly renewal is one record, not one per occurrence made by hand (the Roadmap's f-recurring-dates). A document says `"repeats": true` on a `date` or `datetime` field; a declaration puts `startsAtRepeat: repeatField()` beside `startsAt`, and `toDocument` writes it as `repeats`. A record keeps its first occurrence in the date and, beside it under the date's name and `Repeat`, how it repeats: `{ "every": "week", "on": ["tue"], "until": "2026-12-15" }` — `every` day, week, month or year, an `interval`, `on` weekdays for a week or days of the month for a month (`-1` the last), `until`, `count` and `except` days — or `null` once it no longer does. The store refuses anything else (`validRepeat`). Occurrences are worked out and never stored (`occurrences`, `nextOccurrence`, `dateOn`): a rule, a template, `today()`'s comparisons, a list's sort, filter and date groups, and a kind's stop read the next occurrence on the day they are judged on, so the tick that turns the day (FR-176) moves it with nothing written. A day a month lacks falls on the month's last (Jan 31 monthly is Feb 28, Feb 29 yearly is Feb 28 off a leap year), a moment's time of day is kept as written so six in the evening stays six across a clock change, and `count` counts skipped days. The calendar draws every occurrence in the days it shows, marked ↻ and "· repeats" and not dragged, and counts a series once, at its next, where it is a glance; a kind's stop opens on a calendar by a series' next occurrence and counts it in "in the next 7 days"; a record's facts, a row and a card say "Every Tuesday, next Oct 27" in the app's locale (`repeatAsRead`, `repeatingDateAsRead` on `@graview/core/words`). Three derived acts on a kind with a date that repeats, permitted as its edit is, each one undo with a sentence: `skip-<kind>` ("Skip this one", `{ id, on }`) adds the day to `except`; `detach-<kind>` ("Change just this one") makes that occurrence a record of its own, with the series' fields, the links it makes and what was changed, and an exception on the series; `split-<kind>` ("Change from here") ends the series the day before and starts a new one that day, `continues` naming the old. A record's page has a "Repeat…" control beside the date: a picker (does not repeat, daily, weekly, monthly, yearly; every how many; which weekdays; the month's day or its last; until) that says the rule back in words as it is chosen, every control labeled, kept by the edit act; a form asks for a repeat with the same picker (`FormField` gains `control: "repeat"`). `editDocument` gains `set-repeats`, the studio offers "Say whether it repeats" and writes `repeatField()` back, a migration moves a repeat with its renamed date, `graview types` writes `<date>Repeat?: Repeat | null`, and an act's tool schema describes it with `"x-graview-repeat": true`. Time zones beyond the one a moment says, and the rest of RFC 5545 (by-week-number, by-set-position, hourly), are left out. A hosted page's first load is 5 797 bytes larger minified, 2 381 gzipped, than the batch it joined (549 531 / 192 258 against 543 734 / 189 877, under its budgets of 550 912 / 192 512), and 5 425 / 2 274 handed a compiled app (507 320 / 177 411 against 509 440 / 177 664): the occurrence math, the repeat's check and the three acts, which a rule and the store need as the page loads; the words, the picker and the calendar's drawing are fetched with what draws them. The embed's every face, the studio handed in and the pages face alone are raised by files of their own. A document's `datetime` field now reads back as a date and time (`fieldSpecOf`), so a kind's stop finds it for its calendar and its dates, and the stop says its date by the field's label ("in the next 7 days by starts"). verify-repeats holds it at desk width in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The declaration and check finding codes: the document format gains `repeats` on a date or datetime field, within `graview-document@1` and `graview-compiled@1`, the edit `set-repeats` and the finding `repeat-clash`; a build that predates them refuses the key as `unknown-key`. Derived tool names and input schemas: a kind with a date that repeats gains `skip-<kind>`, `detach-<kind>` and `split-<kind>`, and its `edit-<kind>` takes `<date>Repeat`; a kind with none is unchanged. `ReadableField` gains `repeats`, `PlacedEntry` gains `repeats`, `DerivedAct` gains `skip`, `detach` and `split`. Ops and primitives, the stored formats and the wire are unchanged. What a host does: serve 0.1.29 or later before writing `repeats`; a record's repeat travels as any other field value.
- 6764d85: A district's "⚠ 3" on the map is a control. It was dead text: the name card said a district had problems and nothing on the map said which. It is a button now, a fingertip tall, named for what it opens ("3 problems in Tasks"), and its hover says the first problem in the rule's own sentence. Pressed, or Enter or Space on it, it chooses the district and brings its problems up at the head of the panel beside the map (the sheet on a phone): the bar's own rows, each in the words the problems list uses, with the number of ways to fix it, and a press that chooses the record it names and opens its acts — the repairs lead. The count is the bar's source, the broken rules that name one of the district's records, so the card, the panel and the bar never say two things; the records they name are outlined on the map while the panel is open, and the keyboard goes back to the count when the panel is put away, never to the page. A district's member count is a plain number everywhere — it says "36 rooms" on hover — and is never painted as the warning: the flat district cards on the ground and a group's header used to show "⚠ 36" on a district with one problem, because some member was flagged. They say "36" and, apart from it, the same "⚠ 1" button, which from the ground rises to the map with the problems open. A row's "+n" says what it is on hover. The panel's problems section has a proper heading with a visible focus, and a problem's sentence is said once, with how many ways to fix it beneath.
  
  A district's name is not moved by hand from altitude. The city is laid out by the data and a name stands under its own plot; a drag that starts on a name is the ground's own gesture, the one empty ground makes, and pans the city, plot and name together; it writes no pin. The name is still pressed to choose it and double-pressed to go in. A `pin.kind:<kind>` an old bookmark carries is ignored from altitude, and no plot wears the pinned curb for one. A record's card is still moved by hand, and a district's pin on the ground is as it was.
  
  The panel beside the map floats over it: a surface inset from the right edge, the top (under the bar) and the foot by the app's gap, with the app's radius on every corner, its own edge and the real elevation, rather than a column running flush to the picture's edges. What it holds is clipped to the radius. The city is still laid out in the room left of it — `--graview-dock` is now the panel's width and the gap on each side of it, and a phone's `--graview-sheet` its height, the gap and the safe-area inset — so no district, name or control stands under it.
  
  The scene is painted the whole picture, and the panel floats over it. The ground, grid, depth and tint run under the panel and show in the gap around it, rather than ending where the panel begins: the scene's box is no longer narrowed by `--graview-dock` or shortened by `--graview-sheet`. What the panel takes from the map is the city's fit and the camera's reach — `LayoutOptions.dock` fits the city in what is left, `Layout.visible` is the part the reader sees (the camera's bounds, the edge signs and the zoom's center are for it), and the zoom control, the edge signs and Up and the Key keep clear of the panel. Pointers over the gap belong to the scene; over the panel, to the panel.
  
  Compatibility: additive. A district card's problem count is a button where it was a span; nothing a host wrote reaches it. A district's pin is ignored from altitude, which moves a district a bookmark had placed back under its plot.
- a5c260f: A hosted page loads thirteen chunks before it draws where it loaded twenty-three, and 7.6 KB less (5.0 KB gzipped), so the round's money formats have room after its repeating dates and field-level sight. A bundler splits what a page loads first by which of its doors (each part fetched with `import()`) reach each file, so the frame's files were spread over twenty-three chunks, each with its own list of names in and out and each compressed alone. Every door in `@graview/core`, `@graview/react` and `@graview/primitives` now imports the barrel the page drew first (`@graview/core`, `@graview/react/provider`, `@graview/primitives/frame`) for nothing but that, which makes the files every door reaches one set and costs a door nothing at run time. What draws a notice is fetched with the first notice and prefetched once the page is idle (`notice-stacks.tsx`); the board and the two live regions that say each notice aloud stay up front, so a notice is said at once, and `fetchNotices()` fetches it at will (the embed's `preload` does). What only fetched faces say or do left the barrels a page imports: `edgeWords` and `valueWords` for `@graview/core/words`, and a face's moves (`withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin`, `edgeSelectionId`) for a new `@graview/layout/view-moves`. Built as Cloud builds it over the vendors document, the page that compiles its document goes from 549 727 bytes (192 368 gzipped) to 541 994 (187 254), and the page handed a compiled app from 507 528 (177 639) to 499 794 (172 530). Their budgets fall to keep about 8 KB of room (3.5 KB gzipped): 537.5 KB (186.4 gzipped) from 538.0 (188.0), and 496.0 KB (172.0 gzipped) from 497.5 (173.5). No face loses anything: the pages face alone is 3.4 KB less, and every face fetched together 4.4 KB less.
  
  Compatibility: BREAKING imports. `edgeWords` and `valueWords` move from `@graview/core` to `@graview/core/words`; `withShown`, `withQuery`, `withoutSearch`, `withPast`, `withPan`, `withPin` and `edgeSelectionId` move from `@graview/layout` and `@graview/layout/view` to `@graview/layout/view-moves`; `FOOT_MOVED`, `FOOT_OBSTACLES`, `NARROW_PICTURE`, `placeAtTheFoot` and `placeAtTheTop` leave `@graview/primitives/frame` (still on `@graview/primitives` and `@graview/primitives/pages`). `withoutMoves` stays on `@graview/layout/view`. New: `@graview/layout/view-moves`, and `fetchNotices` from `@graview/primitives`, `/frame` and `/pages`. Ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool names and input schemas are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: import those names from their new entries; its shell built from these sources loads about 7.5 KB less before the app draws.
- 3accacb: A kind opens to something useful. Gone into, a district the app drew no picture of — "open", a double press, `#focus=aggregate:<kind>` — drew six chips and "+30 more" in a box floating in an empty field, under a "⚠ 36" that was the number of rooms in the warning tone: nothing said what the records were, what was wrong with them or what to do. It opens now to the kind's stop, in the box a lens fills, derived from the declaration and the records. Its head says the plural, how many and what they are, and offers the kind's creating act first ("Add a room"), asked in place, with the rest behind "Acts…". Under it, only where there is something to say: how many are overdue or due in the next seven days on its due date, each a press that narrows the list; its problems counted by the rule that found them ("1 problem in rooms: A workshop fits its room"), each opening the bar's own rows and their repairs, and "Show only the rooms with a problem"; and what was added or changed since this reader last looked. Then the picture its data chooses: a board by its main choice (the lifecycle's field, or one called status, stage, state or phase) up to 300 records; a calendar on its main date where today's month holds records a fortnight long or less; otherwise a table of the kind's glance and its relations, read as the far records' names, an empty value said as a quiet dash ("none" to a screen reader), 50 rows at a time with "Show 50 more". Each row is its record to the scene, as a record is anywhere: pressed, it is chosen; right-clicked, long-pressed or given the menu key, its acts open; its name goes to it and its "⋯", drawn with an edge at rest, opens the same acts. Opened from altitude — a double press on its district, or Enter on it chosen — a kind with no picture opens to its stop in the panel beside the map a lens opens in, taking the place of a lens or another kind's stop already open there (one panel at a time; with a lens open it used to open behind it), its district shut on the map; the address says so, the keyboard lands on its heading, and Back returns to what was open. The other shape is one press away, the stop says the picture is derived from the declaration, and "Keep as a lens" keeps it as the seat keeps a view it drew (`add-lens`). The stop is a region named by its heading, a real table on a desk and rows of words in a narrow box, with 44-pixel targets under a finger and nothing scrolling sideways; it is fetched when a district is first gone into, so a page that never opens one carries a door of about a hundred bytes. `@graview/tools/draft` gains `recipeDraft(recipe, options)`, a draft from a recipe with no ask, titled and judged as an asked-for one ("Every room" for a plain list). The graview-lists skill says what a kind opens to and what to declare first, and `pnpm verify kind-stop` walks every example's kinds with no picture at a desk and a phone, light and dark.
  
  Compatibility: from altitude, opening a district with no picture now focuses it (`#overview=1&focus=aggregate:<kind>`) and draws its stop in `[data-testid="lens-panel"]`, where it used to choose it (`sel=kind:<kind>`, the district panel), which a single press still does; `LayoutOptions.besideTheMap` (set by the scene where a panel is drawn) keeps a focused group's district shut and stamps nothing in the city's middle. A view an app registers for a kind at many × full replaces the stop as before; the framework's default for that cell is now the stop, fetched lazily, drawing the old list until it arrives. New test ids are `kind-stop`, `kind-stop-primary`, `kind-stop-acts`, `kind-stop-problems`, `kind-stop-as-<shape>`, `kind-stop-keep`, `kind-stop-table`, `kind-stop-row-go`, `kind-stop-row-acts`, `kind-stop-more` and the arranging line `kind-arrange-*`, and the table's rows carry `data-graview-pick` as a record's mark does; `@graview/tools/draft` adds `recipeDraft`. The embed's every-face budget is raised by the stop's chunk (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kind-opens-to-something-useful-every-face.json`); the hosted page's up-front budget is unchanged.
- cd5a9d5: A lens beside the map is named once, and the studio's "open" opens a district again. The panel a lens opens in from altitude names it in its head, and a lens titles its own panel with the label it is handed, so the todo app's "The lists" stood twice on one screen a hundred pixels apart; a `Panel` whose title is the name the frame above it already says now leaves the title, and the count beside it, to the frame. And the studio, opened inside a Shell or an embed's scene face, inherited their panel beside the map through the portal: "open" on a district chose it for a panel the studio does not draw, and nothing opened. The studio says it draws no such panel, so a district opens in place as a card of its records, as it did before the panel arrived.
- 3cd624d: A lens is listed once, however many kinds it draws.
